```
# Real time meshing of an animated scene with a block predicate.
#
# The scene is the one of the last cusvna example (examples/05_levelset.py and examples/cuda/levelset.cu
# in https://github.com/keckj/cusvna): a gyroid ball surrounded by a ring of animated level sets
# (heart, Kummer and Barth surfaces, tetrahedral quartic, twisted box and metaballs), standing above
# a checkered floor. There the level sets are ray marched with OptiX and cusvna interval marching,
# here their zero level set is extracted by cupmb at every frame and rendered with OpenGL.
#
# The shapes are written once as templates on the evaluation type: they are evaluated on floats for
# the scalar field and on cusvna affine forms cusvna::AF1<float,3> for the block predicate, which
# discards the empty blocks of the octree before any scalar is evaluated, and on first order autodiff
# numbers (https://github.com/keckj/autodiff, git submodule ext/autodiff) for the exact normals at the
# vertices. Each object lives in its own bounding box, the scalar field is the union (min) of the
# objects and is positive outside of all the bounding boxes. The floor is not meshed, it is drawn as
# a plane by the viewer.
#
# Vertices, triangles, normals and colors (vertex properties) are written directly into OpenGL
# buffers (Cuda/OpenGL interoperability, see 03_glinterop_cupy.py). Run with --no-display for a
# headless benchmark of the meshing with and without predicate.
#
# Viewer controls: SPACE pause, +/- animation speed, * and / to multiply or divide the resolution by two
# (the number of cells is a power of two on each axis, the grid size is a kernel argument so that the
# kernels are not recompiled), W wireframe, Q or ESC to quit. They are shown in an overlay if pyimgui is
# installed.
#
# The cost of the predicate scales with the surface (N^2 blocks are refined) while the cost of the
# naive approach scales with the volume (N^3 cells are classified): the predicate is slower at small
# resolutions (N < 2048 on a RTX 5090, where meshing takes a few milliseconds anyway) and pays off at
# high resolutions (1.3x faster at N=2560, 2.7x at N=5120, the default of the benchmark).
#
import os
import sys
import enum
import ctypes
import argparse
import dataclasses

try:
    from cuda.bindings import runtime as cudart  # cuda-python >= 12.6
except ImportError:
    from cuda import cudart  # older cuda-python

if "CUPMB_LOGLEVEL" not in os.environ:
    os.environ["CUPMB_LOGLEVEL"] = "INFO"

import numpy as np
import cupy as cp

from cupmb import (
    ParallelMarchingBlocks,
    Backend,
    BufferType,
    VertexProperty,
    AutotuningRigor,
    logger,
)
from cupmb.utils.units import time2str


# ---------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------
# Scene (same objects and animation as make_scene() in examples/05_levelset.py of cusvna)
# ---------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------
class Shape(enum.IntEnum):
    """Must match the Shape enum of the device code below (same values as cusvna's levelset.cu)."""

    GYROID = 1
    METABALLS = 2
    TWISTED_BOX = 3
    KUMMER = 4
    BARTH = 5
    HEART = 6
    TETRAHEDRAL = 7


ALBEDO = {
    Shape.GYROID: (1.00, 0.55, 0.10),
    Shape.METABALLS: (0.90, 0.15, 0.45),
    Shape.TWISTED_BOX: (0.05, 0.65, 0.60),
    Shape.KUMMER: (0.20, 0.35, 0.95),
    Shape.BARTH: (0.45, 0.80, 0.15),
    Shape.HEART: (0.85, 0.05, 0.08),
    Shape.TETRAHEDRAL: (0.55, 0.25, 0.85),
}

FLOOR_Y = -1.5


def make_scene():
    """A gyroid ball surrounded by a ring of animated level sets: (center, scale, spin, shape, phase)."""
    objects = [((0.0, FLOOR_Y + 1.45, 0.0), 1.4, 0.25, Shape.GYROID, 0.0)]
    # around the gyroid, starting in front of the camera
    ring = [Shape.HEART, Shape.KUMMER, Shape.BARTH, Shape.TETRAHEDRAL, Shape.TWISTED_BOX, Shape.METABALLS]
    ring_radius, ring_scale = 4.0, 0.95
    for i, shape in enumerate(ring):
        angle = np.pi / 2 + 2 * np.pi * (i + 0.5) / len(ring)
        center = (ring_radius * np.cos(angle), FLOOR_Y + ring_scale + 0.1, -ring_radius * np.sin(angle))
        objects.append((center, ring_scale, 0.5 * (-1) ** i, shape, 1.7 * i))
    return objects


def f32(x):
    """Float literal that is parsed to the exact same float32 value on the device."""
    return f"{float(np.float32(x))!r}f"


def make_object_table(objects, albedo):
    """
    Device table of the objects. The shape of an object lives in [-scale, scale]^3 around its
    center whatever its rotation around the y axis: lo/hi is its world bounding box, computed once
    here in single precision so that the scalar field and the predicate test the exact same box.
    """
    rows = []
    for center, scale, spin, shape, phase in objects:
        c = np.asarray(center, dtype=np.float32)
        lo, hi = c - np.float32(scale), c + np.float32(scale)
        rows.append(
            "        {"
            + f"{{{', '.join(map(f32, c))}}}, {f32(scale)}, {f32(spin)}, {f32(phase)}, {shape.name}, "
            + f"{{{', '.join(map(f32, lo))}}}, {{{', '.join(map(f32, hi))}}}, "
            + f"{{{', '.join(map(f32, albedo[shape]))}}}"
            + "},"
        )
    rows = "\n".join(rows)
    return rf"""
namespace levelset {{
    constexpr int object_count = {len(objects)};
    __constant__ Object objects[object_count] = {{
{rows}
    }};
}}
"""


def scene_domain(objects, margin=0.02):
    """Bounding box of all the objects (the floor is not meshed)."""
    lo = np.min([np.asarray(c) - s for (c, s, *_) in objects], axis=0) - margin
    hi = np.max([np.asarray(c) + s for (c, s, *_) in objects], axis=0) + margin
    return lo, hi


# ---------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------
# Device code
# ---------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------

# The level sets are written once as templates on the evaluation type V (float, cusvna affine forms or
# autodiff numbers), the functions of affine forms and autodiff numbers are found by argument dependent
# lookup in namespaces cusvna and autodiff, their float counterparts and the ones missing in autodiff are
# declared here. Shapes are given in local coordinates, they are negative inside and fit in the cylinder
# x^2+z^2 <= 1, |y| <= 1. The animation time t only enters through float coefficients.
AUTODIFF_INCLUDE_DIR = os.path.realpath(
    os.path.join(os.path.dirname(os.path.abspath(__file__)), "..", "ext", "autodiff")
)

PRELUDE = r"""
#include <autodiff/forward/real.hpp>

namespace levelset {
    using real = autodiff::Real<1, float>;
    __device__ inline float sqr(float x) { return x*x; }
    __device__ inline float abs(float x) { return fabsf(x); }
    __device__ inline float min(float x, float y) { return fminf(x, y); }
    __device__ inline float max(float x, float y) { return fmaxf(x, y); }
    __device__ inline float sin(float x) { return sinf(x); }
    __device__ inline float cos(float x) { return cosf(x); }
    __device__ inline float sqrt(float x) { return sqrtf(x); }
    __device__ inline float cbrt(float x) { return cbrtf(x); }
    __device__ inline float positive_part(float x) { return fmaxf(x, 0.0f); }
    __device__ inline real sqr(const real& x) { return x*x; }
    __device__ inline real positive_part(const real& x) { return max(x, 0.0f); }

    template <typename X, typename Y, typename Z>
    __device__ auto length(const X& x, const Y& y, const Z& z) {
        return sqrt(sqr(x) + sqr(y) + sqr(z));
    }
} /* end of namespace levelset */
"""

# Level sets of the scene, ported from examples/cuda/levelset.cu of cusvna (same shapes, same animation).
# A scene defines the Shape enum and visit_shape() in namespace levelset.
SHAPES = r"""
namespace levelset {
    /* a ball carved by a flowing gyroid lattice */
    template <typename V>
    __device__ auto gyroid(const V& x, const V& y, const V& z, const float t) {
        const float k = 6.0f;
        const auto X = k*x;
        const auto Y = k*y - 0.8f*t;
        const auto Z = k*z;
        const auto g = sin(X)*cos(Y) + sin(Y)*cos(Z) + sin(Z)*cos(X);
        const float thickness = 0.35f + 0.25f*sinf(0.7f*t);
        const auto lattice = (abs(g) - thickness) * (1.0f/k);
        const auto ball = length(x, y, z) - 0.95f;
        return max(lattice, ball);
    }

    /* five soft objects with compact polynomial falloff, w(d) = (1 - d^2/R^2)^2 for d < R */
    template <typename V>
    __device__ auto metaballs(const V& x, const V& y, const V& z, const float t) {
        auto blob = [&](const float a, const float b, const float c, const float phase, const float R) {
            const float cx = 0.3f * sinf(a*t + phase);
            const float cy = 0.3f * cosf(b*t + 2.0f*phase);
            const float cz = 0.3f * sinf(c*t + 3.0f*phase);
            const auto d2 = sqr(x - cx) + sqr(y - cy) + sqr(z - cz);
            return sqr(positive_part(1.0f - d2*(1.0f/(R*R))));
        };
        return 0.25f - (blob(1.1f, 0.7f, 0.9f, 0.0f, 0.44f) + blob(0.6f, 1.3f, 0.5f, 1.3f, 0.40f)
                     + blob(0.9f, 0.4f, 1.4f, 2.6f, 0.36f) + blob(1.7f, 0.9f, 0.3f, 3.9f, 0.32f)
                     + blob(0.3f, 1.6f, 1.1f, 5.2f, 0.34f));
    }

    /* a rounded box twisted around the y axis */
    template <typename V>
    __device__ auto twisted_box(const V& x, const V& y, const V& z, const float t) {
        const float twist = 2.2f*sinf(0.6f*t);
        const auto a = twist*y;
        const auto c = cos(a);
        const auto s = sin(a);
        const auto u = c*x - s*z;
        const auto w = s*x + c*z;
        const float r = 0.12f;
        const auto qx = abs(u) - 0.3f;
        const auto qy = abs(y) - 0.78f;
        const auto qz = abs(w) - 0.3f;
        const auto outside = length(positive_part(qx), positive_part(qy), positive_part(qz));
        const auto inside = min(max(qx, max(qy, qz)), 0.0f);
        return outside + inside - r;
    }

    /* Kummer quartic surface clipped by a ball, mu morphs it around its 16 nodes configuration (mu = 1.3) */
    template <typename V>
    __device__ auto kummer(const V& x0, const V& y0, const V& z0, const float t) {
        const float k = 2.4f;
        const auto x = k*x0;
        const auto y = k*z0;
        const auto z = k*y0;
        const float mu2 = sqr(1.3f + 0.2f*sinf(0.5f*t));
        const float lambda = (3.0f*mu2 - 1.0f) / (3.0f - mu2);
        const float s2 = 1.41421356f;
        const auto p = 1.0f - z - s2*x;
        const auto q = 1.0f - z + s2*x;
        const auto r = 1.0f + z + s2*y;
        const auto s = 1.0f + z - s2*y;
        const auto f = sqr(sqr(x) + sqr(y) + sqr(z) - mu2) - lambda*((p*q)*(r*s));
        const auto ball = sqr(x) + sqr(y) + sqr(z) - sqr(0.95f*k);
        return max(f, ball);
    }

    /* Barth sextic (65 nodes for w = 1) clipped by a ball, w breathes around 1 */
    template <typename V>
    __device__ auto barth(const V& x0, const V& y0, const V& z0, const float t) {
        const float k = 1.9f;
        const auto x = k*x0;
        const auto y = k*y0;
        const auto z = k*z0;
        const float phi = 1.61803399f;
        const float phi2 = phi*phi;
        const float w = 1.0f + 0.12f*sinf(0.8f*t);
        const auto xx = sqr(x);
        const auto yy = sqr(y);
        const auto zz = sqr(z);
        const auto f = 4.0f*((phi2*xx - yy)*(phi2*yy - zz))*(phi2*zz - xx)
                     - (1.0f + 2.0f*phi)*sqr(xx + yy + zz - w*w);
        const auto ball = xx + yy + zz - sqr(0.95f*k);
        return max(-f, ball);
    }

    /* Taubin heart surface (x^2 + 9/4 y^2 + z^2 - 1)^3 - z^3 (x^2 + 9/80 y^2) = 0, beating. The cube root
       of the equation has the same sign and a gradient that does not vanish on the surface where z = 0. */
    template <typename V>
    __device__ auto heart(const V& x0, const V& y0, const V& z0, const float t) {
        const float beat = 1.0f + 0.12f*powf(fabsf(sinf(2.5f*t)), 6.0f);
        const float k = 1.35f/beat;
        const auto x = k*x0;
        const auto y = k*z0;
        const auto z = k*y0 + 0.1f;
        return sqr(x) + 2.25f*sqr(y) + sqr(z) - 1.0f - z*cbrt(sqr(x) + 0.1125f*sqr(y));
    }

    /* the tetrahedral quartic r^4 + 8xyz - 10r^2 + c = 0, its handles open and close with c */
    template <typename V>
    __device__ auto tetrahedral(const V& x0, const V& y0, const V& z0, const float t) {
        const float k = 4.0f;
        const auto x = k*x0;
        const auto y = k*y0;
        const auto z = k*z0;
        const float c = 20.0f + 4.0f*sinf(0.6f*t);
        const auto r2 = sqr(x) + sqr(y) + sqr(z);
        return sqr(r2) + 8.0f*((x*y)*z) - 10.0f*r2 + c;
    }

    enum Shape : int {
        GYROID      = 1,
        METABALLS   = 2,
        TWISTED_BOX = 3,
        KUMMER      = 4,
        BARTH       = 5,
        HEART       = 6,
        TETRAHEDRAL = 7,
    };

    /* calls visitor(f) where f(x, y, z) is the level set function of the given shape at time t */
    template <typename Visitor>
    __device__ void visit_shape(const int shape, const float t, Visitor&& visitor) {
        switch (shape) {
            case GYROID:
                visitor([t](const auto& x, const auto& y, const auto& z) { return gyroid(x, y, z, t); });
                break;
            case METABALLS:
                visitor([t](const auto& x, const auto& y, const auto& z) { return metaballs(x, y, z, t); });
                break;
            case TWISTED_BOX:
                visitor([t](const auto& x, const auto& y, const auto& z) { return twisted_box(x, y, z, t); });
                break;
            case KUMMER:
                visitor([t](const auto& x, const auto& y, const auto& z) { return kummer(x, y, z, t); });
                break;
            case BARTH:
                visitor([t](const auto& x, const auto& y, const auto& z) { return barth(x, y, z, t); });
                break;
            case HEART:
                visitor([t](const auto& x, const auto& y, const auto& z) { return heart(x, y, z, t); });
                break;
            case TETRAHEDRAL:
                visitor([t](const auto& x, const auto& y, const auto& z) { return tetrahedral(x, y, z, t); });
                break;
            default:
                break;
        }
    }
} /* end of namespace levelset */
"""

# Objects of the scene: shape, placement and animation.
OBJECTS = r"""
namespace levelset {
    struct Object {
        float center[3];
        float scale;    /* the shape lives in [-scale, scale]^3 around center, in any rotation around y */
        float spin;     /* angular velocity around the y axis */
        float phase;    /* animation time offset */
        int   shape;
        float lo[3];    /* world bounding box */
        float hi[3];
        float albedo[3];
    };

    /* Rotation of an object around its vertical axis at time t, maps world coordinates to local
       coordinates. The mapping is affine so that it is exact for affine forms, and it is differentiated
       along with the shape by autodiff. */
    struct Frame {
        float cx, cy, cz, inv_scale, c, s;

        __device__ Frame(const Object& object, const float t)
            : cx(object.center[0]), cy(object.center[1]), cz(object.center[2]), inv_scale(1.0f/object.scale) {
            sincosf(object.spin * t, &s, &c);
        }

        /* evaluates the local function f at world coordinates (x, y, z) */
        template <typename V, typename F>
        __device__ auto eval(const F& f, const V& x, const V& y, const V& z) const {
            const auto dx = x - cx;
            const auto dy = y - cy;
            const auto dz = z - cz;
            return f(inv_scale*(c*dx - s*dz), inv_scale*dy, inv_scale*(s*dx + c*dz));
        }
    };
} /* end of namespace levelset */
"""

# The scalar field, the normals and the colors. The extra kernel parameter t is the animation time.
FIELD = r"""
namespace levelset {
    /* index of the object whose bounding box contains (x, y, z), -1 if there is none (boxes are disjoint) */
    __device__ inline int find_object(const float x, const float y, const float z) {
        for (int i = 0; i < object_count; ++i) {
            const Object& o = objects[i];
            if ((x >= o.lo[0]) && (x <= o.hi[0]) && (y >= o.lo[1]) && (y <= o.hi[1])
                    && (z >= o.lo[2]) && (z <= o.hi[2]))
                return i;
        }
        return -1;
    }

    /* exact gradient of f at (x, y, z), by forward automatic differentiation (one evaluation per direction) */
    template <typename F>
    __device__ void gradient(const F& f, const float x0, const float y0, const float z0,
                             float& nx, float& ny, float& nz) {
        using autodiff::at;
        using autodiff::derivative;
        using autodiff::wrt;
        real x = x0, y = y0, z = z0;
        nx = derivative(f, wrt(x), at(x, y, z));
        ny = derivative(f, wrt(y), at(x, y, z));
        nz = derivative(f, wrt(z), at(x, y, z));
    }
}

namespace cupmb {
    /* union of the objects, positive outside of all the bounding boxes */
    template <typename T, typename I, I dim>
    __device__ __forceinline__ T scalar(
            const trove::array<T,dim>& p,
            const trove::array<I,dim>& i,
            const T& time) {
        using namespace levelset;
        const T x = trove::get<0>(p);
        const T y = trove::get<1>(p);
        const T z = trove::get<2>(p);
        const int k = find_object(x, y, z);
        T value = T(1);
        if (k >= 0) {
            const Object& o = objects[k];
            const float t = time + o.phase;
            const Frame frame(o, t);
            visit_shape(o.shape, t, [&](const auto& f) { value = frame.eval(f, x, y, z); });
        }
        return value;
    }

    /* normals point from negative to positive values (outward), exact gradient of the scalar field in
       world coordinates computed by autodiff through the frame of the object (as in levelset.cu) */
    template <typename T, typename I, I dim>
    __device__ __forceinline__ trove::array<T,dim> compute_normal(
            const trove::array<T,dim>& p,
            const T& time) {
        using namespace levelset;
        const T x = trove::get<0>(p);
        const T y = trove::get<1>(p);
        const T z = trove::get<2>(p);
        const int k = find_object(x, y, z);
        float nx = 0.0f, ny = 1.0f, nz = 0.0f;
        if (k >= 0) {
            const Object& o = objects[k];
            const float t = time + o.phase;
            const Frame frame(o, t);
            visit_shape(o.shape, t, [&](const auto& f) {
                const auto g = [&](const real& u, const real& v, const real& w) { return frame.eval(f, u, v, w); };
                gradient(g, x, y, z, nx, ny, nz);
            });
            const float n = rnorm3df(nx, ny, nz);
            nx *= n; ny *= n; nz *= n;
        }
        return trove::make_array<T>(nx, ny, nz);
    }

    template <typename T, typename I, I dim>
    __device__ __forceinline__ trove::array<T,dim> compute_color(
            const trove::array<T,dim>& p,
            const T& time) {
        using namespace levelset;
        const int k = find_object(trove::get<0>(p), trove::get<1>(p), trove::get<2>(p));
        if (k < 0)
            return trove::make_array<T>(0.8f, 0.8f, 0.8f);
        const Object& o = objects[k];
        return trove::make_array<T>(o.albedo[0], o.albedo[1], o.albedo[2]);
    }
}
"""

# Predicate definition (pdef): returns false only if the block certainly does not contain any zero of the
# scalar field. A block can only contain zeros inside the bounding box of an object, the shape of the
# object is then evaluated with affine arithmetic on the intersection of the block with the box. This is
# much tighter than a range analysis of the union: only the objects whose box meets the block are
# evaluated, on the part of the block that they cover. domain.a[k] and domain.b[k] are the lower and
# upper bounds of the block along axis k (0=x, 1=y, 2=z).
PDEF = r"""
#include <cusvna/af1.cuh>

namespace cupmb {
    template <typename T, typename I, I dim, typename... F>
    __device__ __forceinline__ bool may_contain_zeros(const BoundingBox<T,dim>& domain, const T& time) {
        using namespace levelset;
        using Interval = cusvna::Interval<float>;
        using AF = cusvna::AF1<float,3>;  /* 3 input noise symbols: x, y and z */

        for (int i = 0; i < object_count; ++i) {
            const Object& o = objects[i];
            const float xa = fmaxf(domain.a[0], o.lo[0]), xb = fminf(domain.b[0], o.hi[0]);
            const float ya = fmaxf(domain.a[1], o.lo[1]), yb = fminf(domain.b[1], o.hi[1]);
            const float za = fmaxf(domain.a[2], o.lo[2]), zb = fminf(domain.b[2], o.hi[2]);
            if ((xa > xb) || (ya > yb) || (za > zb))
                continue;

            const AF x = AF::template from_interval<0>(Interval(xa, xb));
            const AF y = AF::template from_interval<1>(Interval(ya, yb));
            const AF z = AF::template from_interval<2>(Interval(za, zb));

            const float t = time + o.phase;
            const Frame frame(o, t);
            bool zero = false;
            visit_shape(o.shape, t, [&](const auto& f) { zero = possibly_zero(frame.eval(f, x, y, z)); });
            if (zero)
                return true;
        }
        return false;
    }
}
"""

NORMAL = VertexProperty("normal", fname="compute_normal", dtype="T", shape=("dim",))
COLOR = VertexProperty("color", fname="compute_color", dtype="T", shape=("dim",))


@dataclasses.dataclass
class Scene:
    """
    Level sets meshed by make_pmb() and shown by the viewer: device code of the shapes (Shape enum and
    visit_shape() in namespace levelset), objects (center, scale, spin, shape, phase) with their colors,
    extra build options, camera and default grid size of the benchmark.
    """

    title: str
    shapes: str
    objects: list
    albedo: dict
    build_options: tuple = ()
    eye: tuple = (0.0, 6.0, -12.5)
    target: tuple = (0.0, -0.6, 0.0)
    benchmark_N: int = 5120


SCENE = Scene("cupmb real time level sets", SHAPES, make_scene(), ALBEDO)


def make_pmb(scene, N, predicate, rigor, pow2=False):
    """
    Marching blocks on the bounding box of the scene, with cubic cells and N cells along x. With pow2, the
    number of cells on each axis is rounded up to a power of two and the domain is centered on the scene.
    """
    objects = scene.objects
    lo, hi = scene_domain(objects)
    L = hi - lo
    dx = L[0] / N
    Ns = np.ceil(L / dx).astype(np.int64)
    if pow2:
        Ns = 1 << np.ceil(np.log2(Ns)).astype(np.int64)
        lo = (lo + hi) / 2 - Ns * dx / 2
    return ParallelMarchingBlocks(
        3,
        PRELUDE + scene.shapes + OBJECTS + make_object_table(objects, scene.albedo) + FIELD,
        pdef=(PDEF if predicate else ""),
        x0=lo,
        L=Ns * dx,
        N=Ns,
        ftype=np.float32,
        itype=np.uint32,
        backend=Backend.CUPY,
        extra_params=[("t", "T")],
        vertex_properties=(NORMAL, COLOR),
        autotuning_rigor=rigor,
        enable_cuda_std_lib=True,
        # Disable FMA contraction so that the scalar field is evaluated identically by all kernel
        # variants: the triangle counts with and without predicate can then be compared exactly.
        extra_build_options=("--fmad=false", f"-I{AUTODIFF_INCLUDE_DIR}") + tuple(scene.build_options),
    )


# ---------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------
# Headless benchmark
# ---------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------
def benchmark(pmb, times):
    """Meshes the scene at the given animation times, returns the meshing times and triangle counts."""
    pmb(t=float(times[0]))  # build, autotune and preallocate buffers
    elapsed, triangles = [], []
    for t in times:
        pmb(t=float(t))  # (re)allocate the buffers if needed, the second call is timed
        (V, I, *_), dt = pmb(t=float(t), return_elapsed_time=True)
        elapsed.append(dt)
        triangles.append(0 if I is None else int(I.shape[0]))
    return np.asarray(elapsed), np.asarray(triangles)


def run_benchmark(scene, args):
    times = np.linspace(0.0, 30.0, args.frames, endpoint=False) + 0.123
    rigor = AutotuningRigor[args.rigor]
    results = {}
    for predicate in (False,) if args.no_predicate else (False, True):
        pmb = make_pmb(scene, args.N, predicate, rigor)
        elapsed, triangles = benchmark(pmb, times)
        name = "with" if predicate else "without"
        logger.info(
            f"N={tuple(map(int, pmb.N))}: median meshing time {time2str(float(np.median(elapsed)))} {name} predicate "
            f"({len(times)} frames, {int(triangles.min())} to {int(triangles.max())} triangles)."
        )
        results[predicate] = (elapsed, triangles)
        del pmb

    if args.no_predicate:
        return 0

    (t0, n0), (t1, n1) = results[False], results[True]
    logger.info(f"Speedup factor is {np.median(t0) / np.median(t1):2.1f} (ratio of the median meshing times).")
    mismatch = np.flatnonzero(n0 != n1)
    if mismatch.size > 0:
        for k in mismatch:
            logger.error(f"Triangle count mismatch at t={times[k]:.3f}: {n0[k]} without predicate vs {n1[k]} with.")
        return 1
    logger.info("Triangle counts are identical with and without predicate on all frames.")
    return 0


# ---------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------
# OpenGL viewer
# ---------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------

# Shading of levelset.cu without shadows: sky, sun light, hemispheric ambient, Blinn-Phong highlights,
# Fresnel reflection of the sky and distance fog.
GLSL_SHADING = """
uniform vec3 eye;

const vec3 sun_direction = normalize(vec3(-0.45, 0.75, -0.48));

vec3 sky(vec3 dir) {
    float h = max(dir.y, 0.0);
    vec3 color = mix(vec3(0.85, 0.72, 0.62), vec3(0.12, 0.25, 0.55), pow(h, 0.5));
    float sd = max(dot(dir, sun_direction), 0.0);
    color += vec3(1.0, 0.75, 0.45) * (0.25*pow(sd, 8.0) + 2.0*pow(sd, 512.0));
    if (dir.y < 0.0)
        color = mix(color, vec3(0.35, 0.3, 0.28), min(-4.0*dir.y, 1.0));
    return color;
}

vec3 to_srgb(vec3 c) {
    c = clamp(c, 0.0, 1.0);
    return mix(12.92*c, 1.055*pow(c, vec3(1.0/2.4)) - 0.055, step(0.0031308, c));
}

vec3 shade(vec3 p, vec3 normal, vec3 color, float shininess, bool reflective) {
    vec3 V = normalize(eye - p);
    vec3 H = normalize(sun_direction + V);
    float ndotl = dot(normal, sun_direction);
    vec3 sun_color = vec3(1.0, 0.92, 0.8) * 2.2;
    vec3 ambient = mix(vec3(0.25, 0.2, 0.18), vec3(0.3, 0.4, 0.6), 0.5 + 0.5*normal.y);
    float fresnel = 0.04 + 0.96*pow(1.0 - max(dot(normal, V), 0.0), 5.0);
    float specular = (ndotl > 0.0 ? 1.0 : 0.0) * pow(max(dot(normal, H), 0.0), shininess) * (shininess + 8.0) / 25.0;
    vec3 radiance = color * (ambient + sun_color * max(ndotl, 0.0)) + sun_color * specular * 0.25;
    if (reflective)
        radiance += fresnel * 0.5 * sky(reflect(-V, normal));
    float d = length(p - eye);
    float fog = 1.0 - exp(-0.0015 * d * d);
    return to_srgb(mix(radiance, sky(-V), fog));
}
"""

MESH_VS = """
#version 330
layout(location = 0) in vec3 position;
layout(location = 1) in vec3 normal;
layout(location = 2) in vec3 color;
uniform mat4 view_projection;
out vec3 v_position;
out vec3 v_normal;
out vec3 v_color;
void main() {
    v_position = position;
    v_normal = normal;
    v_color = color;
    gl_Position = view_projection * vec4(position, 1.0);
}
"""

MESH_FS = (
    """
#version 330
in vec3 v_position;
in vec3 v_normal;
in vec3 v_color;
out vec4 out_color;
"""
    + GLSL_SHADING
    + """
void main() {
    vec3 n = normalize(v_normal);
    vec3 color = v_color;
    /* two sided surfaces: clipped algebraic surfaces show their back faces, tint them */
    if (dot(n, eye - v_position) < 0.0) {
        n = -n;
        color = mix(color, vec3(1.0, 0.95, 0.85), 0.6);
    }
    out_color = vec4(shade(v_position, n, color, 64.0, true), 1.0);
}
"""
)

FLOOR_VS = """
#version 330
layout(location = 0) in vec3 position;
uniform mat4 view_projection;
out vec3 v_position;
void main() {
    v_position = position;
    gl_Position = view_projection * vec4(position, 1.0);
}
"""

FLOOR_FS = (
    """
#version 330
in vec3 v_position;
out vec4 out_color;
"""
    + GLSL_SHADING
    + """
void main() {
    float checker = float((int(floor(v_position.x)) + int(floor(v_position.z))) & 1);
    vec3 color = mix(vec3(0.55, 0.53, 0.50), vec3(0.75, 0.73, 0.70), checker);
    out_color = vec4(shade(v_position, vec3(0.0, 1.0, 0.0), color, 16.0, false), 1.0);
}
"""
)

SKY_VS = """
#version 330
layout(location = 0) in vec2 position;
out vec2 v_ndc;
void main() {
    v_ndc = position;
    gl_Position = vec4(position, 1.0, 1.0);
}
"""

SKY_FS = (
    """
#version 330
in vec2 v_ndc;
out vec4 out_color;
uniform mat4 inv_view_projection;
"""
    + GLSL_SHADING
    + """
void main() {
    vec4 p = inv_view_projection * vec4(v_ndc, 1.0, 1.0);
    out_color = vec4(to_srgb(sky(normalize(p.xyz/p.w - eye))), 1.0);
}
"""
)


def perspective(fov_y, aspect, near, far):
    f = 1.0 / np.tan(np.radians(fov_y) / 2)
    return np.array(
        [
            [f / aspect, 0, 0, 0],
            [0, f, 0, 0],
            [0, 0, (far + near) / (near - far), 2 * far * near / (near - far)],
            [0, 0, -1, 0],
        ],
        dtype=np.float32,
    )


def look_at(eye, target, up=(0.0, 1.0, 0.0)):
    w = eye - target
    w /= np.linalg.norm(w)
    u = np.cross(up, w)
    u /= np.linalg.norm(u)
    v = np.cross(w, u)
    M = np.eye(4, dtype=np.float32)
    M[0, :3], M[1, :3], M[2, :3] = u, v, w
    M[:3, 3] = -M[:3, :3] @ eye
    return M


class OrbitCamera:
    """Camera orbiting around a target point: left button drag to rotate, wheel to zoom."""

    def __init__(self, eye=(0.0, 6.0, -12.5), target=(0.0, -0.6, 0.0), fov_y=35.0):
        self.target = np.asarray(target, dtype=np.float32)
        offset = np.asarray(eye, dtype=np.float32) - self.target
        self.distance = float(np.linalg.norm(offset))
        self.pitch = float(np.arcsin(offset[1] / self.distance))
        self.yaw = float(np.arctan2(offset[0], -offset[2]))
        self.fov_y = fov_y

    @property
    def eye(self):
        cp, sp = np.cos(self.pitch), np.sin(self.pitch)
        offset = self.distance * np.array([cp * np.sin(self.yaw), sp, -cp * np.cos(self.yaw)], dtype=np.float32)
        return self.target + offset

    def rotate(self, dx, dy):
        self.yaw += 0.005 * dx
        self.pitch = float(np.clip(self.pitch + 0.005 * dy, -1.5, 1.5))

    def zoom(self, steps):
        self.distance = float(np.clip(self.distance * 0.9**steps, 2.0, 60.0))

    def view_projection(self, aspect):
        return perspective(self.fov_y, aspect, 0.1, 200.0) @ look_at(self.eye, self.target)


# Bounds of the resolution of the viewer. The number of cells is a power of two on each axis (the domain is
# extended around the scene) and the '*' and '/' keys multiply or divide it by two. The grid size is a kernel
# argument: changing the resolution reallocates the buffers but does not recompile the kernels.
N_MIN, N_MAX = 64, 4096
VIEWER_N = 2048  # default resolution of the viewer

CONTROLS = """\
SPACE   pause
+/-     animation speed (or UP/DOWN)
* /     resolution x2, /2 (or PAGE UP/DOWN)
W       wireframe
mouse   left drag to rotate, wheel to zoom
Q/ESC   quit"""


def run_viewer(scene, args):
    import glfw
    from OpenGL import GL
    from OpenGL.GL import shaders

    try:
        import imgui
        from imgui.integrations.glfw import GlfwRenderer
    except ImportError:
        imgui = None  # no overlay, the controls are logged and the settings are shown in the window title

    predicate = not args.no_predicate
    # powers of two on each axis (the domain is extended around the scene)
    N = int(np.clip(1 << round(np.log2(args.N)), N_MIN, N_MAX))
    if N != args.N:
        logger.info(f"Viewer resolutions are powers of two, using N={N} instead of N={args.N}.")
    pmb = make_pmb(scene, N, predicate, AutotuningRigor[args.rigor], pow2=True)

    if not glfw.init():
        logger.error("Could not initialize glfw.")
        return 1
    glfw.window_hint(glfw.SAMPLES, 4)
    title = f"{scene.title} (predicate {'on' if predicate else 'off'})"
    width, height = map(int, args.size.lower().split("x"))
    window = glfw.create_window(width, height, title, None, None)
    if not window:
        glfw.terminate()
        logger.error("Could not create a glfw window.")
        return 1
    glfw.make_context_current(window)
    glfw.swap_interval(0)

    if imgui is not None:
        imgui.create_context()
        overlay = GlfwRenderer(window, attach_callbacks=False)  # the callbacks of the viewer are set below
        imgui.get_style().window_border_size = 0.0

    def program(vs, fs):
        return shaders.compileProgram(
            shaders.compileShader(vs, GL.GL_VERTEX_SHADER), shaders.compileShader(fs, GL.GL_FRAGMENT_SHADER)
        )

    mesh_program = program(MESH_VS, MESH_FS)
    floor_program = program(FLOOR_VS, FLOOR_FS)
    sky_program = program(SKY_VS, SKY_FS)

    def static_vao(data, components):
        vao = GL.glGenVertexArrays(1)
        GL.glBindVertexArray(vao)
        vbo = GL.glGenBuffers(1)
        GL.glBindBuffer(GL.GL_ARRAY_BUFFER, vbo)
        GL.glBufferData(GL.GL_ARRAY_BUFFER, data.nbytes, data, GL.GL_STATIC_DRAW)
        GL.glEnableVertexAttribArray(0)
        GL.glVertexAttribPointer(0, components, GL.GL_FLOAT, GL.GL_FALSE, 0, ctypes.c_void_p(0))
        return vao

    s = 60.0
    floor_vao = static_vao(
        np.array([[-s, FLOOR_Y, -s], [s, FLOOR_Y, -s], [s, FLOOR_Y, s], [-s, FLOOR_Y, s]], dtype=np.float32), 3
    )
    sky_vao = static_vao(np.array([[-1, -1], [3, -1], [-1, 3]], dtype=np.float32), 2)

    # OpenGL buffers shared with cupmb: vertices, normals and colors (3 floats each) and triangles (3 uint32).
    flags = cudart.cudaGraphicsRegisterFlags.cudaGraphicsRegisterFlagsWriteDiscard
    mesh_vao = GL.glGenVertexArrays(1)
    GL.glBindVertexArray(mesh_vao)

    class SharedBuffer:
        def __init__(self, key, target, location=None):
            self.key, self.target, self.location = key, target, location
            self.gl_buffer = GL.glGenBuffers(1)
            self.cuda_buffer = None
            self.capacity = 0

        def resize(self, capacity):
            if self.cuda_buffer is not None:
                self.cuda_buffer.unregister()
            GL.glBindVertexArray(mesh_vao)
            GL.glBindBuffer(self.target, self.gl_buffer)
            GL.glBufferData(self.target, 12 * capacity, None, GL.GL_DYNAMIC_DRAW)
            if self.location is not None:
                GL.glEnableVertexAttribArray(self.location)
                GL.glVertexAttribPointer(self.location, 3, GL.GL_FLOAT, GL.GL_FALSE, 0, ctypes.c_void_p(0))
            self.cuda_buffer = pmb.backend.CudaOpenGLMappedBuffer(self.gl_buffer, flags)
            self.capacity = capacity

    vertex_buffers = [
        SharedBuffer(BufferType.VERTICES, GL.GL_ARRAY_BUFFER, 0),
        SharedBuffer(NORMAL, GL.GL_ARRAY_BUFFER, 1),
        SharedBuffer(COLOR, GL.GL_ARRAY_BUFFER, 2),
    ]
    index_buffer = SharedBuffer(BufferType.SIMPLICES, GL.GL_ELEMENT_ARRAY_BUFFER)
    shared_buffers = vertex_buffers + [index_buffer]

    def mesh(t):
        """Meshes the scene at time t directly into the OpenGL buffers, grows them if they are too small."""
        for buf in shared_buffers:
            pmb.replace_buffer(buf.key, buf.cuda_buffer.map())
        try:
            (V, I, *_), elapsed = pmb(t=t, return_elapsed_time=True)
        finally:
            for buf in shared_buffers:
                buf.cuda_buffer.unmap()
        nv = 0 if V is None else int(V.shape[0])
        nt = 0 if I is None else int(I.shape[0])
        if (nv > vertex_buffers[0].capacity) or (nt > index_buffer.capacity):
            # cupmb wrote the mesh into its own (larger) buffers instead, grow the OpenGL ones and retry
            logger.info(f"Growing OpenGL buffers to {3 * nv // 2} vertices and {3 * nt // 2} triangles.")
            for buf in vertex_buffers:
                buf.resize(3 * nv // 2)
            index_buffer.resize(3 * nt // 2)
            return mesh(t)
        return nt, elapsed

    for buf in shared_buffers:
        buf.resize(1 << 20)

    camera = OrbitCamera(scene.eye, scene.target)
    state = dict(paused=bool(args.screenshot), wireframe=args.wireframe, drag=None, speed=1.0, N=N, requested_N=None)

    def change_speed(factor):
        state["speed"] = float(np.clip(state["speed"] * factor, 1.0 / 64, 64.0))

    def change_resolution(up):
        N = state["requested_N"] or state["N"]
        N = min(max(2 * N if up else N // 2, N_MIN), N_MAX)
        state["requested_N"] = None if N == state["N"] else N

    def rebuild(N):
        """Replaces the marching blocks instance by a new one with N cells along x (estimated block sizes)."""
        nonlocal pmb
        logger.info(f"Rebuilding the marching blocks for N={N}...")
        pmb = None  # releases the buffers of the previous instance before allocating the new ones
        cp.get_default_memory_pool().free_all_blocks()
        pmb = make_pmb(scene, N, predicate, AutotuningRigor.ESTIMATE, pow2=True)
        state["N"] = N

    def on_key(window, key, scancode, action, mods):
        if action == glfw.RELEASE:
            return
        if key in (glfw.KEY_UP, glfw.KEY_DOWN):  # repeated while held
            change_speed(1.25 if key == glfw.KEY_UP else 1 / 1.25)
        elif action != glfw.PRESS:
            return
        elif key == glfw.KEY_ESCAPE:
            glfw.set_window_should_close(window, True)
        elif key == glfw.KEY_SPACE:
            state["paused"] = not state["paused"]
        elif key in (glfw.KEY_PAGE_UP, glfw.KEY_PAGE_DOWN):
            change_resolution(key == glfw.KEY_PAGE_UP)

    def on_char(window, codepoint):
        # letters and symbols are handled as characters: glfw key codes are physical US positions (KEY_W is Z
        # on AZERTY, '+' and '*' are typed with different keys depending on the layout)
        c = chr(codepoint).lower()
        if c == "q":
            glfw.set_window_should_close(window, True)
        elif c == "w":
            state["wireframe"] = not state["wireframe"]
        elif c in "+=":
            change_speed(1.25)
        elif c == "-":
            change_speed(1 / 1.25)
        elif c == "*":
            change_resolution(True)
        elif c == "/":
            change_resolution(False)

    def on_mouse_button(window, button, action, mods):
        if button == glfw.MOUSE_BUTTON_LEFT:
            state["drag"] = glfw.get_cursor_pos(window) if action == glfw.PRESS else None

    def on_cursor(window, x, y):
        if state["drag"] is not None:
            x0, y0 = state["drag"]
            camera.rotate(x - x0, y - y0)
            state["drag"] = (x, y)

    def on_scroll(window, dx, dy):
        camera.zoom(dy)

    glfw.set_key_callback(window, on_key)
    glfw.set_char_callback(window, on_char)
    glfw.set_mouse_button_callback(window, on_mouse_button)
    glfw.set_cursor_pos_callback(window, on_cursor)
    glfw.set_scroll_callback(window, on_scroll)

    logger.info("Controls:\n" + CONTROLS)

    GL.glEnable(GL.GL_DEPTH_TEST)
    GL.glEnable(GL.GL_MULTISAMPLE)

    logger.info("Building the kernels and meshing the first frame...")
    mesh(args.time)

    def settings(requested_N):
        paused = " (paused)" if state["paused"] else ""
        lines = [
            f"N        : {' x '.join(map(str, map(int, pmb.N)))} cells",
            f"predicate: {'on' if predicate else 'off'}",
            f"speed    : x{state['speed']:.2f}{paused}",
        ]
        if requested_N is not None:
            lines.append(f"building the kernels for N={requested_N}...")
        return "\n".join(lines)

    def draw_overlay(text):
        overlay.process_inputs()
        imgui.new_frame()
        imgui.set_next_window_bg_alpha(0.4)
        imgui.set_next_window_position(10.0, 10.0)
        imgui.begin(
            "Overlay",
            None,
            imgui.WINDOW_NO_TITLE_BAR
            | imgui.WINDOW_NO_RESIZE
            | imgui.WINDOW_NO_MOVE
            | imgui.WINDOW_NO_SCROLLBAR
            | imgui.WINDOW_NO_SAVED_SETTINGS
            | imgui.WINDOW_NO_INPUTS
            | imgui.WINDOW_ALWAYS_AUTO_RESIZE,
        )
        imgui.text(text)
        imgui.end()
        imgui.render()
        overlay.render(imgui.get_draw_data())

    anim_time = args.time
    last = start = glfw.get_time()
    stats_start, stats_frames, stats_meshing = last, 0, 0.0
    stats_text = "fps      : -\nmeshing  : -\ntriangles: -"
    frames, total_meshing = 0, 0.0
    while not glfw.window_should_close(window):
        now = glfw.get_time()
        if not state["paused"]:
            anim_time += (now - last) * state["speed"]
        last = now

        ntriangles, elapsed = mesh(anim_time)
        stats_frames += 1
        stats_meshing += elapsed
        total_meshing += elapsed

        if now - stats_start >= 0.5:
            fps = stats_frames / (now - stats_start)
            meshing = stats_meshing / stats_frames
            stats_text = f"fps      : {fps:.0f}\nmeshing  : {1e3 * meshing:.1f} ms\ntriangles: {ntriangles / 1e6:.2f}M"
            glfw.set_window_title(
                window,
                f"{title} - N={state['N']} - {fps:.0f} fps - meshing {1e3 * meshing:.1f} ms - "
                f"{ntriangles / 1e6:.2f}M triangles - speed x{state['speed']:.2f}"
                + (" (paused)" if state["paused"] else ""),
            )
            stats_start, stats_frames, stats_meshing = now, 0, 0.0

        # a new resolution is applied after the frame announcing it has been displayed
        requested_N = state["requested_N"]

        width, height = glfw.get_framebuffer_size(window)
        if width == 0 or height == 0:
            glfw.poll_events()
            continue
        GL.glViewport(0, 0, width, height)
        GL.glClear(GL.GL_COLOR_BUFFER_BIT | GL.GL_DEPTH_BUFFER_BIT)

        eye = camera.eye
        VP = camera.view_projection(width / height)

        # the sky lies on the far plane, where it would fail the depth test against the cleared depth buffer
        GL.glPolygonMode(GL.GL_FRONT_AND_BACK, GL.GL_FILL)
        GL.glDisable(GL.GL_DEPTH_TEST)
        GL.glUseProgram(sky_program)
        GL.glUniformMatrix4fv(
            GL.glGetUniformLocation(sky_program, "inv_view_projection"), 1, GL.GL_TRUE, np.linalg.inv(VP)
        )
        GL.glUniform3fv(GL.glGetUniformLocation(sky_program, "eye"), 1, eye)
        GL.glBindVertexArray(sky_vao)
        GL.glDrawArrays(GL.GL_TRIANGLES, 0, 3)
        GL.glEnable(GL.GL_DEPTH_TEST)

        for prog in (floor_program, mesh_program):
            GL.glUseProgram(prog)
            GL.glUniformMatrix4fv(GL.glGetUniformLocation(prog, "view_projection"), 1, GL.GL_TRUE, VP)
            GL.glUniform3fv(GL.glGetUniformLocation(prog, "eye"), 1, eye)

        GL.glUseProgram(floor_program)
        GL.glBindVertexArray(floor_vao)
        GL.glDrawArrays(GL.GL_TRIANGLE_FAN, 0, 4)

        if state["wireframe"]:
            GL.glPolygonMode(GL.GL_FRONT_AND_BACK, GL.GL_LINE)
        GL.glUseProgram(mesh_program)
        GL.glBindVertexArray(mesh_vao)
        GL.glDrawElements(GL.GL_TRIANGLES, 3 * ntriangles, GL.GL_UNSIGNED_INT, None)

        if args.screenshot:
            from PIL import Image

            GL.glPixelStorei(GL.GL_PACK_ALIGNMENT, 1)
            pixels = GL.glReadPixels(0, 0, width, height, GL.GL_RGB, GL.GL_UNSIGNED_BYTE)
            Image.fromarray(np.frombuffer(pixels, dtype=np.uint8).reshape(height, width, 3)[::-1]).save(args.screenshot)
            logger.info(f"Screenshot saved to {args.screenshot} ({ntriangles} triangles).")
            break

        if imgui is not None:
            GL.glPolygonMode(GL.GL_FRONT_AND_BACK, GL.GL_FILL)
            draw_overlay(f"{stats_text}\n\n{settings(requested_N)}\n\n{CONTROLS}")
        elif requested_N is not None:
            glfw.set_window_title(window, f"{title} - building the kernels for N={requested_N}...")

        glfw.swap_buffers(window)
        glfw.poll_events()
        frames += 1

        if requested_N is not None:
            N, state["requested_N"] = state["requested_N"], None
            if N is not None:
                previous_N = state["N"]
                try:
                    rebuild(N)
                    mesh(anim_time)
                except cp.cuda.memory.OutOfMemoryError as e:
                    logger.error(f"Not enough device memory for N={N} ({e}), going back to N={previous_N}.")
                    rebuild(previous_N)
            # the build time is neither animated nor accounted in the statistics
            last = stats_start = glfw.get_time()
            stats_frames, stats_meshing = 0, 0.0

        if args.frames and frames >= args.frames:
            break

    if frames > 0:
        fps = frames / (glfw.get_time() - start)
        logger.info(f"{frames} frames, {fps:.1f} fps on average, mean meshing time {time2str(total_meshing / frames)}.")
    if imgui is not None:
        overlay.shutdown()
    for buf in shared_buffers:
        buf.cuda_buffer.unregister()
    glfw.terminate()
    return 0


def main(scene, description):
    parser = argparse.ArgumentParser(description=description)
    parser.add_argument(
        "-N",
        type=int,
        help=f"number of grid cells along x (the longest axis), rounded to a power of two by the viewer "
        f"(default: {VIEWER_N} for the viewer, {scene.benchmark_N} for the benchmark)",
    )
    parser.add_argument("--no-predicate", action="store_true", help="disable the block predicate")
    parser.add_argument(
        "--rigor",
        default="ESTIMATE",
        choices=[r.name for r in AutotuningRigor],
        help="autotuning rigor",
    )
    parser.add_argument(
        "--frames",
        type=int,
        default=0,
        help="number of frames (default: 32 with --no-display, unlimited otherwise)",
    )
    parser.add_argument(
        "--no-display",
        action="store_true",
        help="headless benchmark: meshes the scene at different times with and without predicate",
    )
    parser.add_argument("--time", type=float, default=0.0, help="animation time of the first frame")
    parser.add_argument("--size", default="1280x720", help="size of the viewer window (WIDTHxHEIGHT)")
    parser.add_argument("--wireframe", action="store_true", help="starts the viewer in wireframe mode")
    parser.add_argument("--screenshot", metavar="FILE", help="saves the first frame of the viewer to FILE and quits")
    args = parser.parse_args()

    if not os.path.isdir(os.path.join(AUTODIFF_INCLUDE_DIR, "autodiff")):
        logger.error("Cannot find the autodiff submodule, run git submodule update --init --recursive.")
        return 1

    if args.no_display:
        args.frames = args.frames or 32
        args.N = args.N or scene.benchmark_N
        return run_benchmark(scene, args)
    args.N = args.N or VIEWER_N
    return run_viewer(scene, args)


if __name__ == "__main__":
    sys.exit(main(SCENE, "Real time meshing of animated level sets with a block predicate."))
```

### examples/09_eigen_levelset.py
```
#
# Copyright (c) 2021-2026 Jean-Baptiste Keck <jbkeck@hotmail.com>
# SPDX-License-Identifier: MIT
#

# Real time meshing of level sets written with Eigen matrices.
#
# The scene is the one of the Eigen example of cusvna (examples/06_eigen_levelset.py and
# examples/cuda/eigen_levelset.cu in https://github.com/keckj/cusvna): a Menger sponge between a
# spectrahedron and a trefoil knot. The shapes are written with Eigen vectors and matrices (rotations,
# quaternions, Householder reflections, determinants, quadratic forms) of floats for the scalar field,
# of cusvna affine forms cusvna::AF1<float,3> for the block predicate and of autodiff numbers for the
# normals. Eigen is compiled by NVRTC with the eigen-nvrtc git submodule (ext/eigen-nvrtc).
#
# Only AF1 forms can be Eigen scalars: the type of an AA form records its noise symbols and changes with
# each nonlinear operation, while the coefficients of an Eigen matrix share the same type.
#
# Same viewer, benchmark and options as 08_levelset.py.
#
import os
import sys
import enum
import importlib

script_dir = os.path.dirname(os.path.abspath(__file__))
sys.path.insert(0, script_dir)
levelset = importlib.import_module("08_levelset")

EIGEN_INCLUDE_DIR = os.path.realpath(os.path.join(script_dir, "..", "ext", "eigen-nvrtc"))


class Shape(enum.IntEnum):
    """Must match the Shape enum of the device code below."""

    MENGER = 1
    SPECTRAHEDRON = 2
    TREFOIL = 3


ALBEDO = {
    Shape.MENGER: (0.95, 0.62, 0.20),
    Shape.SPECTRAHEDRON: (0.10, 0.55, 0.95),
    Shape.TREFOIL: (0.90, 0.15, 0.50),
}


def make_scene():
    """A Menger sponge between a spectrahedron and a trefoil knot: (center, scale, spin, shape, phase)."""
    floor_y = levelset.FLOOR_Y
    return [
        ((0.0, floor_y + 0.97, 0.0), 1.4, 0.2, Shape.MENGER, 0.0),
        ((-3.2, floor_y + 0.67, 0.6), 0.95, -0.4, Shape.SPECTRAHEDRON, 0.0),
        ((3.2, floor_y + 0.9, 0.6), 0.95, 0.3, Shape.TREFOIL, 0.0),
    ]


# Level sets ported from examples/cuda/eigen_levelset.cu of cusvna (same shapes, same animation). They are
# function objects whose call operator is a template on the scalar type V: float, affine form or autodiff
# number. They work on Eigen vectors and matrices of V, mixed with matrices of floats (rotations, folding
# planes) computed by the constructor.
SHAPES = r"""
#include <Eigen/Core>
#include <Eigen/Geometry>
#include <Eigen/LU>

#include <autodiff/forward/real/eigen.hpp>
#include <cusvna/af1.cuh>

/* Eigen matrices of AF1 forms, mixed with matrices of floats in products and coefficient-wise operations.
   autodiff/forward/real/eigen.hpp does the same for autodiff::Real. */
namespace Eigen {
    template <typename T, int N>
    struct NumTraits<cusvna::AF1<T,N>> : NumTraits<T> {
        using Real = cusvna::AF1<T,N>;
        using NonInteger = cusvna::AF1<T,N>;
        using Nested = cusvna::AF1<T,N>;
        using Literal = T;
        enum {
            IsComplex = 0,
            IsInteger = 0,
            IsSigned = 1,
            RequireInitialization = 1,
            ReadCost = 1,
            AddCost = 4,
            MulCost = 8,
        };
    };

    template <typename T, int N, typename BinaryOp>
    struct ScalarBinaryOpTraits<cusvna::AF1<T,N>, T, BinaryOp> { using ReturnType = cusvna::AF1<T,N>; };

    template <typename T, int N, typename BinaryOp>
    struct ScalarBinaryOpTraits<T, cusvna::AF1<T,N>, BinaryOp> { using ReturnType = cusvna::AF1<T,N>; };
} /* end of namespace Eigen */

namespace levelset {
    template <typename V>
    using Vector3 = Eigen::Matrix<V, 3, 1>;

    template <typename V>
    __device__ V max_norm(const Vector3<V>& p) {
        return max(max(abs(p.x()), abs(p.y())), abs(p.z()));
    }

    /* Menger sponge as a kaleidoscopic iterated function system: each iteration folds space with
       p = |p| and three Householder reflections that sort the coordinates, rotates it and scales it
       around a corner of the cube, which maps the 20 subcubes of the sponge to the cube. The rotation
       slowly moves away from the identity (the classical sponge) and back. */
    struct Menger {
        static constexpr int iterations = 3;
        static constexpr float size = 0.68f;  /* half side of the cube, its corners fit in the unit cylinder */
        Eigen::Matrix3f R;

        __device__ explicit Menger(const float t) {
            const Eigen::Quaternionf q(Eigen::AngleAxisf(0.55f, Eigen::Vector3f(1.0f, 2.0f, 3.0f).normalized()));
            R = Eigen::Quaternionf::Identity().slerp(0.5f - 0.5f*cosf(0.4f*t), q).toRotationMatrix();
        }

        template <typename V>
        __device__ V operator()(const V& x, const V& y, const V& z) const {
            const float h = 0.70710678f;
            const Eigen::Vector3f folds[3] = {{h, -h, 0.0f}, {h, 0.0f, -h}, {0.0f, h, -h}};

            Vector3<V> p(x, y, z);
            p *= 1.0f/size;
            const V cube = max_norm(p) - 1.0f;
            for (int i = 0; i < iterations; ++i) {
                p = p.cwiseAbs();
                for (const Eigen::Vector3f& n : folds)
                    p -= (2.0f*min(n.dot(p), 0.0f)) * n;
                p = R*p;
                p = 3.0f*p - Eigen::Vector3f(2.0f, 2.0f, 0.0f);
                p.z() = 1.0f - abs(p.z() - 1.0f);
            }
            const float scale = 1.0f / 27.0f;  /* 3^-iterations */
            return size * max((max_norm(p) - 1.0f) * scale, cube);
        }
    };

    /* A spectrahedron is the set where a symmetric matrix depending linearly on the coordinates is positive
       definite. For A = [[1, x, y, z], [x, 1, l*z, l*y], [y, l*z, 1, l*x], [z, l*y, l*x, 1]] it is the unit
       ball for l = 0 and the tetrahedron whose faces are the eigenvalues 1 +/- x +/- y +/- z of A for
       l = +/-1, l oscillates between them. A is positive definite if and only if its leading principal
       minors are positive (Sylvester's criterion), the smallest one is the level set function. */
    struct Spectrahedron {
        float lambda;

        __device__ explicit Spectrahedron(const float t) : lambda(sinf(0.5f*t)) {}

        template <typename V>
        __device__ V operator()(const V& x0, const V& y0, const V& z0) const {
            const float k = 1.45f;  /* the vertices (+/-1, +/-1, +/-1) of the tetrahedra fit in the unit cylinder */
            const V x = k*x0;
            const V y = k*y0;
            const V z = k*z0;
            const V one(1.0f);
            const V lx = lambda*x;
            const V ly = lambda*y;
            const V lz = lambda*z;
            Eigen::Matrix<V, 4, 4> A;
            A << one,   x,   y,   z,
                   x, one,  lz,  ly,
                   y,  lz, one,  lx,
                   z,  ly,  lx, one;
            const V m2 = A.template topLeftCorner<2, 2>().determinant();
            const V m3 = A.template topLeftCorner<3, 3>().determinant();
            const V m4 = A.determinant();
            return -min(min(m2, m3), m4);
        }
    };

    /* Ellipsoidal beads sliding along a trefoil knot, with their main axis along the knot and twisting
       around it. Each bead is the quadratic form |D R^T (p - c)|^2 < 1 where the rotation R is given by a
       quaternion and D is the diagonal of the inverse radii, the union is the minimum of the forms.
       (Quaternion::FromTwoVectors would be shorter, but it falls back to a JacobiSVD that calls malloc
       for opposite vectors.) */
    struct Trefoil {
        static constexpr int beads = 24;
        float t;

        __device__ explicit Trefoil(const float t) : t(t) {}

        template <typename V>
        __device__ V operator()(const V& x, const V& y, const V& z) const {
            const float k = 0.27f;
            const Eigen::Vector3f inv_radii(1.0f/0.2f, 1.0f/0.12f, 1.0f/0.06f);
            const Vector3<V> p(x, y, z);
            V d;
            for (int i = 0; i < beads; ++i) {
                const float s = (6.28318531f*i + 0.6f*t) / beads;
                float s1, c1, s2, c2, s3, c3;
                sincosf(s, &s1, &c1);
                sincosf(2.0f*s, &s2, &c2);
                sincosf(3.0f*s, &s3, &c3);
                const Eigen::Vector3f c = k*Eigen::Vector3f(s1 + 2.0f*s2, c1 - 2.0f*c2, -s3);
                /* the tangent is never vertical: the norm of its (x, y) part |exp(-is) + 4 exp(2is)| is at least 3 */
                const Eigen::Vector3f tangent = Eigen::Vector3f(c1 + 4.0f*c2, -s1 + 4.0f*s2, -3.0f*c3).normalized();
                const Eigen::Vector3f normal = tangent.cross(Eigen::Vector3f::UnitZ()).normalized();
                Eigen::Matrix3f frame;
                frame << tangent, normal, tangent.cross(normal);
                const Eigen::Quaternionf q = Eigen::Quaternionf(frame)
                                           * Eigen::AngleAxisf(3.0f*s + 1.5f*t, Eigen::Vector3f::UnitX());
                const Eigen::Matrix3f Rt = q.toRotationMatrix().transpose();
                const V e = (Rt*(p - c)).cwiseProduct(inv_radii).squaredNorm() - 1.0f;
                d = (i == 0 ? e : V(min(d, e)));
            }
            return d;
        }
    };

    enum Shape : int {
        MENGER        = 1,
        SPECTRAHEDRON = 2,
        TREFOIL       = 3,
    };

    /* calls visitor(f) where f(x, y, z) is the level set function of the given shape at time t */
    template <typename Visitor>
    __device__ void visit_shape(const int shape, const float t, Visitor&& visitor) {
        switch (shape) {
            case MENGER:
                visitor(Menger(t));
                break;
            case SPECTRAHEDRON:
                visitor(Spectrahedron(t));
                break;
            case TREFOIL:
                visitor(Trefoil(t));
                break;
            default:
                break;
        }
    }
} /* end of namespace levelset */
"""

# Eigen leaves many functions without __host__ __device__ annotations, NVRTC compiles them as device
# functions with -default-device.
SCENE = levelset.Scene(
    "cupmb Eigen level sets",
    SHAPES,
    make_scene(),
    ALBEDO,
    build_options=("-default-device", f"-I{EIGEN_INCLUDE_DIR}"),
    eye=(0.0, 3.2, -8.5),
    target=(0.0, -0.7, 0.3),
    benchmark_N=2560,
)

if __name__ == "__main__":
    if not os.path.isdir(os.path.join(EIGEN_INCLUDE_DIR, "Eigen")):
        levelset.logger.error("Cannot find the eigen-nvrtc submodule, run git submodule update --init --recursive.")
        sys.exit(1)
    sys.exit(levelset.main(SCENE, "Real time meshing of level sets written with Eigen matrices."))
```

### examples/10_gamma.py
```
#
# Copyright (c) 2021-2026 Jean-Baptiste Keck <jbkeck@hotmail.com>
# SPDX-License-Identifier: MIT
#

# One-dimensional example: root finding on a very fine grid with a block predicate.
#
# The gamma function has no zero, we look instead for the solutions of Gamma(x) = exp(k*pi)
# for x > 0 and any integer k, that is the zeros of f(x) = sin(lgamma(x)). Among them x=1 and
# x=2 (Gamma=1) are exact. The other ones are computed on the host by bisection to check the results.
#
# The default grid has 2^32 cells on [2^-8, 10^6] (64 bit indices), it contains about 4 million roots.
# The roots are sparse on such a grid: the predicate, evaluated with the affine arithmetic
# library cusvna (https://github.com/keckj/cusvna, lgamma is only defined for x > 0), discards almost
# all the blocks of the binary tree before any scalar is evaluated.
#
import os
import math
import argparse

if "CUPMB_LOGLEVEL" not in os.environ:
    os.environ["CUPMB_LOGLEVEL"] = "INFO"

import numpy as np

from cupmb import ParallelMarchingBlocks, AllocationPolicy, logger
from cupmb.utils.units import time2str

# Scalar field definition (fdef): a templated function S f<T>(S x) where S is either T (float/double)
# or an affine form, lgamma and sin are found by argument dependent lookup for affine forms.
fdef = r"""
namespace cupmb {
    template <typename T, typename S>
    __device__ __forceinline__ S f(const S& x) {
        return sin(lgamma(x));
    }

    template <typename T, typename I, I dim>
    __device__ __forceinline__ T scalar(
            const trove::array<T,dim>& p,
            const trove::array<I,dim>& i) {
        return f<T>(trove::get<0>(p));
    }
}
"""

# Predicate definition (pdef): returns false only if the block [a, b] certainly does not contain any zero of f.
pdef = r"""
#include <cusvna/af1.cuh>

namespace cupmb {
    template <typename T, typename I, I dim>
    __device__ __forceinline__ bool may_contain_zeros(const BoundingBox<T,dim>& domain) {
        using Interval = cusvna::Interval<T>;
        using AF = cusvna::AF1<T,1>;  /* 1 input noise symbol: x */
        const AF x = AF::template from_interval<0>(Interval(domain.a[0], domain.b[0]));
        return possibly_zero(f<T>(x));
    }
}
"""

XMIN = 1.4616321449683623  # minimum of the gamma function on x > 0, lgamma(XMIN) > -pi


def bisect(g, a, b, iterations=200):
    """Zero of a monotonic function g on [a, b] with g(a) and g(b) of opposite signs."""
    ga = g(a)
    for _ in range(iterations):
        m = 0.5 * (a + b)
        if m in (a, b):
            break
        if (g(m) > 0) == (ga > 0):
            a, ga = m, g(m)
        else:
            b = m
    return 0.5 * (a + b)


XSTIRLING = 10.0  # the Stirling series below is used on [XSTIRLING, +inf)


def lgamma_stirling(x):
    """Vectorized lgamma for x >= XSTIRLING: Stirling series, the truncation error is below 1e-15."""
    y = 1.0 / (x * x)
    series = (1 / 12 - y * (1 / 360 - y * (1 / 1260 - y * (1 / 1680 - y / 1188)))) / x
    return (x - 0.5) * np.log(x) - x + 0.5 * math.log(2 * math.pi) + series


def bisect_vectorized(g, a, b, iterations=64):
    """Zeros of the increasing function g on the brackets [a, b] (arrays), g(a) <= 0 <= g(b)."""
    a, b = a.copy(), b.copy()
    for _ in range(iterations):
        m = 0.5 * (a + b)
        positive = g(m) > 0
        b = np.where(positive, m, b)
        a = np.where(positive, a, m)
    return 0.5 * (a + b)


def reference_roots(x0, x1):
    """Solutions of lgamma(x) = k*pi on [x0, x1] with 0 < x0 < 1 and x1 > XSTIRLING: lgamma decreases on
    (0, XMIN] from +inf to lgamma(XMIN) > -pi and increases on [XMIN, +inf). The roots larger than XSTIRLING
    are all computed at once by a vectorized bisection."""
    roots = []
    for k in range(int(math.lgamma(x0) / math.pi), 0, -1):
        roots.append(bisect(lambda x: math.lgamma(x) - k * math.pi, x0, 1.0))
    roots += [1.0, 2.0]
    k_stirling = int(math.lgamma(XSTIRLING) / math.pi) + 1
    for k in range(1, k_stirling):
        roots.append(bisect(lambda x: math.lgamma(x) - k * math.pi, 2.0, XSTIRLING))
    K = np.arange(k_stirling, int(math.lgamma(x1) / math.pi) + 1) * math.pi
    a, b = np.full_like(K, XSTIRLING), np.full_like(K, x1)
    roots += bisect_vectorized(lambda x: lgamma_stirling(x) - K, a, b).tolist()
    return np.asarray(roots)


def benchmark(pmb, runs):
    pmb()  # preallocate buffers (and autotune)
    V, _ = pmb(to_numpy=True)
    times = [pmb(return_elapsed_time=True)[-1] for _ in range(runs)]
    return float(np.median(times)), np.sort(V[:, 0])


if __name__ == "__main__":
    parser = argparse.ArgumentParser(description="Solutions of Gamma(x) = exp(k*pi) with a block predicate.")
    parser.add_argument("-N", type=int, default=2**32, help="grid size")
    parser.add_argument("--x0", type=float, default=2**-8, help="lower bound of the domain (0 < x0 < 1)")
    parser.add_argument("--x1", type=float, default=1e6, help=f"upper bound of the domain (x1 > {XSTIRLING:g})")
    parser.add_argument("--runs", type=int, default=10, help="number of timed runs")
    parser.add_argument("--no-plot", action="store_true", help="do not display the results with matplotlib")
    args = parser.parse_args()

    x0, x1, N = args.x0, args.x1, args.N
    assert 0 < x0 < 1 < XSTIRLING < x1, f"the domain has to contain [1, {XSTIRLING:g}] and be strictly positive"

    pmb = ParallelMarchingBlocks(
        1,
        fdef,
        x0=x0,
        L=x1 - x0,
        N=N,
        ftype=np.float64,
        itype=np.uint64 if N >= 2**32 else np.uint32,  # vertex indices of the grid go up to N
        default_allocation_policy=AllocationPolicy.ALLOCATE_EXACT_SIZE,
        enable_cuda_std_lib=True,
    )

    t0, X0 = benchmark(pmb, args.runs)
    pmb.pdef = pdef
    t1, X1 = benchmark(pmb, args.runs)

    logger.info(f"Kernel launcher finished in {time2str(t0)} without predicate ({X0.size} roots, N={N}).")
    logger.info(f"Kernel launcher finished in {time2str(t1)} with predicate ({X1.size} roots, N={N}).")
    logger.info(f"Speedup factor is {t0/t1:2.1f}.")

    R = reference_roots(x0, x1)
    if not (X0.size == X1.size == R.size):
        logger.error(f"Root count mismatch: {X0.size} without predicate, {X1.size} with predicate, {R.size} expected.")
        raise SystemExit(1)
    if not np.array_equal(X0, X1):
        logger.error("Roots found with and without predicate differ.")
        raise SystemExit(1)

    dx = pmb.dx[0]
    error = np.abs(X1 - R)
    logger.info(
        f"Maximal root error is {error.max():.3e} for a grid step dx={dx:.3e} (x=1: {error[R == 1.0][0]:.1e}, "
        f"x=2: {error[R == 2.0][0]:.1e})."
    )
    if error.max() > dx:
        logger.error("Roots are not within one grid step of the reference solutions.")
        raise SystemExit(1)

    if not args.no_plot:
        import matplotlib.pyplot as plt

        xmax = min(x1, 10.0)
        x = np.linspace(x0, xmax, 4096)
        lg = np.asarray([math.lgamma(xi) for xi in x])
        roots = X1[X1 <= xmax]

        fig, axes = plt.subplots(2, 1, sharex=True, figsize=(10, 7))
        axes[0].semilogy(x, np.exp(lg), "k")
        for k in range(-1, int(lg.max() / math.pi) + 2):
            axes[0].axhline(math.exp(k * math.pi), color="0.8", lw=0.5)
        axes[0].semilogy(roots, [math.gamma(r) for r in roots], "r.")
        axes[0].set_ylabel(r"$\Gamma(x)$ and $e^{k\pi}$")
        axes[1].plot(x, np.sin(lg), "k")
        axes[1].plot(roots, np.zeros_like(roots), "r.")
        axes[1].axhline(0, color="0.8", lw=0.5)
        axes[1].set_xlabel("x")
        axes[1].set_xlim(0, xmax)
        axes[1].set_ylabel(r"$\sin(\ln\Gamma(x))$")
        fig.suptitle(rf"cupmb 1D: solutions of $\Gamma(x)=e^{{k\pi}}$ ({X1.size} roots on [{x0:g}, {x1:g}])")
        plt.show()
```

```
import glob

class Settings:

            # "ibl_rotation":

            "use_sun": True,
        },
        "Less Bright day with sun at +Y": {
            "ibl_intensity": 35000,
            "sun_intensity": 50000,
            "sun_dir": [0.577, -0.577, -0.577],
            # "ibl_rotation":
            "use_ibl": True,
            "use_sun": True,
        },
        "Less Bright day with sun at -Y": {
            "ibl_intensity": 35000,
            "sun_intensity": 50000,
            "sun_dir": [0.577, 0.577, 0.577],
            # "ibl_rotation":
            "use_ibl": True,
            "use_sun": True,
        },
        "Less Bright day with sun at +Z": {
            "ibl_intensity": 35000,
            "sun_intensity": 50000,
            "sun_dir": [0.577, 0.577, -0.577],
            # "ibl_rotation":
            "use_ibl": True,
            "use_sun": True,

            "ibl_intensity": 60000,
            "sun_intensity": 50000,
```
