```
    def __init__(self):
        self.mouse_model = gui.SceneWidget.Controls.ROTATE_CAMERA
        self.bg_color = gui.Color(1, 1, 1)
        self.show_skybox = False
        self.show_axes = False
        self.use_ibl = True
        self.use_sun = True
        self.new_ibl_name = None  # clear to None after loading
        self.ibl_intensity = 45000
        self.sun_intensity = 45000
        self.sun_dir = [0.577, -0.577, -0.577]
        self.sun_color = gui.Color(1, 1, 1)

        self.apply_material = True  # clear to False after processing
        self._materials = {
            Settings.LIT: rendering.MaterialRecord(),
            Settings.UNLIT: rendering.MaterialRecord(),
            Settings.NORMALS: rendering.MaterialRecord(),
            Settings.DEPTH: rendering.MaterialRecord(),
        }
        self._materials[Settings.LIT].base_color = [0.9, 0.9, 0.9, 1.0]
        self._materials[Settings.LIT].shader = Settings.LIT
        self._materials[Settings.UNLIT].base_color = [0.9, 0.9, 0.9, 1.0]
        self._materials[Settings.UNLIT].shader = Settings.UNLIT
        self._materials[Settings.NORMALS].shader = Settings.NORMALS
        self._materials[Settings.DEPTH].shader = Settings.DEPTH

        # Conveniently, assigning from self._materials[...] assigns a reference,
        # not a copy, so if we change the property of a material, then switch
        # to another one, then come back, the old setting will still be there.
        self.material = self._materials[Settings.LIT]

    def set_material(self, name):
        self.material = self._materials[name]
        self.apply_material = True

    def apply_material_prefab(self, name):
        assert self.material.shader == Settings.LIT
        prefab = Settings.PREFAB[name]
        for key, val in prefab.items():
            setattr(self.material, "base_" + key, val)

    def apply_lighting_profile(self, name):
        profile = Settings.LIGHTING_PROFILES[name]
        for key, val in profile.items():
            setattr(self, key, val)


class AppWindow:
    MENU_OPEN = 1
    MENU_EXPORT = 2
    MENU_QUIT = 3
    MENU_SHOW_SETTINGS = 11
    MENU_ABOUT = 21

    DEFAULT_IBL = "default"

    MATERIAL_NAMES = ["Lit", "Unlit", "Normals", "Depth"]
    MATERIAL_SHADERS = [Settings.LIT, Settings.UNLIT, Settings.NORMALS, Settings.DEPTH]

    def __init__(self, width, height):
        self.settings = Settings()
        resource_path = gui.Application.instance.resource_path
        self.settings.new_ibl_name = resource_path + "/" + AppWindow.DEFAULT_IBL

        self.window = gui.Application.instance.create_window("Open3D", width, height)
        w = self.window  # to make the code more concise

        # 3D widget
        self._scene = gui.SceneWidget()
        self._scene.scene = rendering.Open3DScene(w.renderer)
        self._scene.set_on_sun_direction_changed(self._on_sun_dir)

        # ---- Settings panel ----
        # Rather than specifying sizes in pixels, which may vary in size based
        # on the monitor, especially on macOS which has 220 dpi monitors, use
        # the em-size. This way sizings will be proportional to the font size,
        # which will create a more visually consistent size across platforms.
        em = w.theme.font_size
        separation_height = int(round(0.5 * em))

        # Widgets are laid out in layouts: gui.Horiz, gui.Vert,
        # gui.CollapsableVert, and gui.VGrid. By nesting the layouts we can
        # achieve complex designs. Usually we use a vertical layout as the
        # topmost widget, since widgets tend to be organized from top to bottom.
        # Within that, we usually have a series of horizontal layouts for each
        # row. All layouts take a spacing parameter, which is the spacing
        # between items in the widget, and a margins parameter, which specifies
        # the spacing of the left, top, right, bottom margins. (This acts like
        # the 'padding' property in CSS.)
        self._settings_panel = gui.Vert(
            0, gui.Margins(0.25 * em, 0.25 * em, 0.25 * em, 0.25 * em)
        )

        # Create a collapsable vertical widget, which takes up enough vertical
        # space for all its children when open, but only enough for text when
        # closed. This is useful for property pages, so the user can hide sets
        # of properties they rarely use.
        view_ctrls = gui.CollapsableVert(
            "View controls", 0.25 * em, gui.Margins(em, 0, 0, 0)
        )

        self._arcball_button = gui.Button("Arcball")
        self._arcball_button.horizontal_padding_em = 0.5
        self._arcball_button.vertical_padding_em = 0
        self._arcball_button.set_on_clicked(self._set_mouse_mode_rotate)
        self._fly_button = gui.Button("Fly")
        self._fly_button.horizontal_padding_em = 0.5
        self._fly_button.vertical_padding_em = 0
        self._fly_button.set_on_clicked(self._set_mouse_mode_fly)
        self._model_button = gui.Button("Model")
        self._model_button.horizontal_padding_em = 0.5
        self._model_button.vertical_padding_em = 0
        self._model_button.set_on_clicked(self._set_mouse_mode_model)
        self._sun_button = gui.Button("Sun")
        self._sun_button.horizontal_padding_em = 0.5
        self._sun_button.vertical_padding_em = 0
        self._sun_button.set_on_clicked(self._set_mouse_mode_sun)
        self._ibl_button = gui.Button("Environment")
        self._ibl_button.horizontal_padding_em = 0.5
        self._ibl_button.vertical_padding_em = 0
        self._ibl_button.set_on_clicked(self._set_mouse_mode_ibl)
        view_ctrls.add_child(gui.Label("Mouse controls"))
        # We want two rows of buttons, so make two horizontal layouts. We also
        # want the buttons centered, which we can do be putting a stretch item
        # as the first and last item. Stretch items take up as much space as
        # possible, and since there are two, they will each take half the extra
        # space, thus centering the buttons.
        h = gui.Horiz(0.25 * em)  # row 1
        h.add_stretch()
        h.add_child(self._arcball_button)
        h.add_child(self._fly_button)
        h.add_child(self._model_button)
        h.add_stretch()
        view_ctrls.add_child(h)
        h = gui.Horiz(0.25 * em)  # row 2
        h.add_stretch()
        h.add_child(self._sun_button)
        h.add_child(self._ibl_button)
        h.add_stretch()
        view_ctrls.add_child(h)

        self._show_skybox = gui.Checkbox("Show skymap")
        self._show_skybox.set_on_checked(self._on_show_skybox)
        view_ctrls.add_fixed(separation_height)
        view_ctrls.add_child(self._show_skybox)

        self._bg_color = gui.ColorEdit()
        self._bg_color.set_on_value_changed(self._on_bg_color)

        grid = gui.VGrid(2, 0.25 * em)
        grid.add_child(gui.Label("BG Color"))
        grid.add_child(self._bg_color)
        view_ctrls.add_child(grid)

        self._show_axes = gui.Checkbox("Show axes")
        self._show_axes.set_on_checked(self._on_show_axes)
        view_ctrls.add_fixed(separation_height)
        view_ctrls.add_child(self._show_axes)

        self._profiles = gui.Combobox()
        for name in sorted(Settings.LIGHTING_PROFILES.keys()):
            self._profiles.add_item(name)
        self._profiles.add_item(Settings.CUSTOM_PROFILE_NAME)
        self._profiles.set_on_selection_changed(self._on_lighting_profile)
        view_ctrls.add_fixed(separation_height)
        view_ctrls.add_child(gui.Label("Lighting profiles"))
        view_ctrls.add_child(self._profiles)
        self._settings_panel.add_fixed(separation_height)
        self._settings_panel.add_child(view_ctrls)

        advanced = gui.CollapsableVert("Advanced lighting", 0, gui.Margins(em, 0, 0, 0))
        advanced.set_is_open(False)

        self._use_ibl = gui.Checkbox("HDR map")
        self._use_ibl.set_on_checked(self._on_use_ibl)
        self._use_sun = gui.Checkbox("Sun")
        self._use_sun.set_on_checked(self._on_use_sun)
        advanced.add_child(gui.Label("Light sources"))
        h = gui.Horiz(em)
        h.add_child(self._use_ibl)
        h.add_child(self._use_sun)
        advanced.add_child(h)

        self._ibl_map = gui.Combobox()
        for ibl in glob.glob(gui.Application.instance.resource_path + "/*_ibl.ktx"):

            self._ibl_map.add_item(os.path.basename(ibl[:-8]))
        self._ibl_map.selected_text = AppWindow.DEFAULT_IBL
        self._ibl_map.set_on_selection_changed(self._on_new_ibl)
        self._ibl_intensity = gui.Slider(gui.Slider.INT)
        self._ibl_intensity.set_limits(0, 200000)
        self._ibl_intensity.set_on_value_changed(self._on_ibl_intensity)
        grid = gui.VGrid(2, 0.25 * em)
        grid.add_child(gui.Label("HDR map"))
        grid.add_child(self._ibl_map)
        grid.add_child(gui.Label("Intensity"))
        grid.add_child(self._ibl_intensity)
        advanced.add_fixed(separation_height)
        advanced.add_child(gui.Label("Environment"))
        advanced.add_child(grid)

        self._sun_intensity = gui.Slider(gui.Slider.INT)
        self._sun_intensity.set_limits(0, 200000)
        self._sun_intensity.set_on_value_changed(self._on_sun_intensity)
        self._sun_dir = gui.VectorEdit()
        self._sun_dir.set_on_value_changed(self._on_sun_dir)
        self._sun_color = gui.ColorEdit()
        self._sun_color.set_on_value_changed(self._on_sun_color)
        grid = gui.VGrid(2, 0.25 * em)
        grid.add_child(gui.Label("Intensity"))
        grid.add_child(self._sun_intensity)
        grid.add_child(gui.Label("Direction"))
        grid.add_child(self._sun_dir)
        grid.add_child(gui.Label("Color"))
        grid.add_child(self._sun_color)
        advanced.add_fixed(separation_height)
        advanced.add_child(gui.Label("Sun (Directional light)"))
        advanced.add_child(grid)

        self._settings_panel.add_fixed(separation_height)
        self._settings_panel.add_child(advanced)

        material_settings = gui.CollapsableVert(
            "Material settings", 0, gui.Margins(em, 0, 0, 0)
        )

        self._shader = gui.Combobox()
        self._shader.add_item(AppWindow.MATERIAL_NAMES[0])
        self._shader.add_item(AppWindow.MATERIAL_NAMES[1])
        self._shader.add_item(AppWindow.MATERIAL_NAMES[2])
        self._shader.add_item(AppWindow.MATERIAL_NAMES[3])
        self._shader.set_on_selection_changed(self._on_shader)
        self._material_prefab = gui.Combobox()
        for prefab_name in sorted(Settings.PREFAB.keys()):
            self._material_prefab.add_item(prefab_name)
        self._material_prefab.selected_text = Settings.DEFAULT_MATERIAL_NAME
        self._material_prefab.set_on_selection_changed(self._on_material_prefab)
        self._material_color = gui.ColorEdit()
        self._material_color.set_on_value_changed(self._on_material_color)
        self._point_size = gui.Slider(gui.Slider.INT)
        self._point_size.set_limits(1, 10)
        self._point_size.set_on_value_changed(self._on_point_size)

        grid = gui.VGrid(2, 0.25 * em)
        grid.add_child(gui.Label("Type"))
        grid.add_child(self._shader)
        grid.add_child(gui.Label("Material"))
        grid.add_child(self._material_prefab)
        grid.add_child(gui.Label("Color"))
        grid.add_child(self._material_color)
        self._point_size_label = gui.Label("Point size")
        grid.add_child(self._point_size_label)
        grid.add_child(self._point_size)
        material_settings.add_child(grid)

        self._settings_panel.add_fixed(separation_height)
        self._settings_panel.add_child(material_settings)
        # ----

        # Normally our user interface can be children of all one layout (usually
        # a vertical layout), which is then the only child of the window. In our
        # case we want the scene to take up all the space and the settings panel
        # to go above it. We can do this custom layout by providing an on_layout
        # callback. The on_layout callback should set the frame
        # (position + size) of every child correctly. After the callback is
        # done the window will layout the grandchildren.
        w.set_on_layout(self._on_layout)
        w.add_child(self._scene)
        w.add_child(self._settings_panel)

        # ---- Menu ----
        # The menu is global (because the macOS menu is global), so only create
        # it once, no matter how many windows are created
        if gui.Application.instance.menubar is None:
            if isMacOS:
                app_menu = gui.Menu()
                app_menu.add_item("About", AppWindow.MENU_ABOUT)
                app_menu.add_separator()
                app_menu.add_item("Quit", AppWindow.MENU_QUIT)
            file_menu = gui.Menu()
            file_menu.add_item("Open...", AppWindow.MENU_OPEN)
            file_menu.add_item("Export Current Image...", AppWindow.MENU_EXPORT)
            if not isMacOS:
                file_menu.add_separator()
                file_menu.add_item("Quit", AppWindow.MENU_QUIT)
            settings_menu = gui.Menu()
            settings_menu.add_item("Lighting & Materials", AppWindow.MENU_SHOW_SETTINGS)
            settings_menu.set_checked(AppWindow.MENU_SHOW_SETTINGS, True)
            help_menu = gui.Menu()
            help_menu.add_item("About", AppWindow.MENU_ABOUT)

            menu = gui.Menu()
            if isMacOS:
                # macOS will name the first menu item for the running application
                # (in our case, probably "Python"), regardless of what we call
                # it. This is the application menu, and it is where the
                # About..., Preferences..., and Quit menu items typically go.
                menu.add_menu("Example", app_menu)
                menu.add_menu("File", file_menu)
                menu.add_menu("Settings", settings_menu)
                # Don't include help menu unless it has something more than
                # About...
            else:
                menu.add_menu("File", file_menu)
                menu.add_menu("Settings", settings_menu)
                menu.add_menu("Help", help_menu)
            gui.Application.instance.menubar = menu

        # The menubar is global, but we need to connect the menu items to the
        # window, so that the window can call the appropriate function when the
        # menu item is activated.
        w.set_on_menu_item_activated(AppWindow.MENU_OPEN, self._on_menu_open)
        w.set_on_menu_item_activated(AppWindow.MENU_EXPORT, self._on_menu_export)
        w.set_on_menu_item_activated(AppWindow.MENU_QUIT, self._on_menu_quit)
        w.set_on_menu_item_activated(
            AppWindow.MENU_SHOW_SETTINGS, self._on_menu_toggle_settings_panel
        )
        w.set_on_menu_item_activated(AppWindow.MENU_ABOUT, self._on_menu_about)
        # ----

        self._apply_settings()

    def _apply_settings(self):
        bg_color = [
            self.settings.bg_color.red,
            self.settings.bg_color.green,
            self.settings.bg_color.blue,
            self.settings.bg_color.alpha,
        ]
        self._scene.scene.set_background(bg_color)
        self._scene.scene.show_skybox(self.settings.show_skybox)
        self._scene.scene.show_axes(self.settings.show_axes)
        if self.settings.new_ibl_name is not None:
            self._scene.scene.scene.set_indirect_light(self.settings.new_ibl_name)
            # Clear new_ibl_name, so we don't keep reloading this image every
            # time the settings are applied.
            self.settings.new_ibl_name = None
        self._scene.scene.scene.enable_indirect_light(self.settings.use_ibl)
        self._scene.scene.scene.set_indirect_light_intensity(
            self.settings.ibl_intensity
        )
        sun_color = [
            self.settings.sun_color.red,
            self.settings.sun_color.green,
            self.settings.sun_color.blue,
        ]
        self._scene.scene.scene.set_sun_light(
            self.settings.sun_dir, sun_color, self.settings.sun_intensity
        )
        self._scene.scene.scene.enable_sun_light(self.settings.use_sun)

        if self.settings.apply_material:
            self._scene.scene.update_material(self.settings.material)
            self.settings.apply_material = False

        self._bg_color.color_value = self.settings.bg_color
        self._show_skybox.checked = self.settings.show_skybox
        self._show_axes.checked = self.settings.show_axes
        self._use_ibl.checked = self.settings.use_ibl
        self._use_sun.checked = self.settings.use_sun
        self._ibl_intensity.int_value = self.settings.ibl_intensity
        self._sun_intensity.int_value = self.settings.sun_intensity
        self._sun_dir.vector_value = self.settings.sun_dir
        self._sun_color.color_value = self.settings.sun_color
        self._material_prefab.enabled = self.settings.material.shader == Settings.LIT
        c = gui.Color(
            self.settings.material.base_color[0],
            self.settings.material.base_color[1],
            self.settings.material.base_color[2],
            self.settings.material.base_color[3],
        )
        self._material_color.color_value = c
        self._point_size.double_value = self.settings.material.point_size

    def _on_layout(self, layout_context):
        # The on_layout callback should set the frame (position + size) of every
        # child correctly. After the callback is done the window will layout
        # the grandchildren.
        r = self.window.content_rect
        self._scene.frame = r
        width = 17 * layout_context.theme.font_size
        height = min(
            r.height,
            self._settings_panel.calc_preferred_size(
                layout_context, gui.Widget.Constraints()
            ).height,
        )
        self._settings_panel.frame = gui.Rect(r.get_right() - width, r.y, width, height)

    def _set_mouse_mode_rotate(self):
        self._scene.set_view_controls(gui.SceneWidget.Controls.ROTATE_CAMERA)

    def _set_mouse_mode_fly(self):
        self._scene.set_view_controls(gui.SceneWidget.Controls.FLY)

    def _set_mouse_mode_sun(self):
        self._scene.set_view_controls(gui.SceneWidget.Controls.ROTATE_SUN)

    def _set_mouse_mode_ibl(self):
        self._scene.set_view_controls(gui.SceneWidget.Controls.ROTATE_IBL)

    def _set_mouse_mode_model(self):
        self._scene.set_view_controls(gui.SceneWidget.Controls.ROTATE_MODEL)

    def _on_bg_color(self, new_color):
        self.settings.bg_color = new_color
        self._apply_settings()

    def _on_show_skybox(self, show):
        self.settings.show_skybox = show
        self._apply_settings()

    def _on_show_axes(self, show):
        self.settings.show_axes = show
        self._apply_settings()

    def _on_use_ibl(self, use):
        self.settings.use_ibl = use
        self._profiles.selected_text = Settings.CUSTOM_PROFILE_NAME
        self._apply_settings()

    def _on_use_sun(self, use):
        self.settings.use_sun = use
        self._profiles.selected_text = Settings.CUSTOM_PROFILE_NAME
        self._apply_settings()

    def _on_lighting_profile(self, name, index):
        if name != Settings.CUSTOM_PROFILE_NAME:
            self.settings.apply_lighting_profile(name)
            self._apply_settings()

    def _on_new_ibl(self, name, index):
        self.settings.new_ibl_name = gui.Application.instance.resource_path + "/" + name
        self._profiles.selected_text = Settings.CUSTOM_PROFILE_NAME
        self._apply_settings()

    def _on_ibl_intensity(self, intensity):
        self.settings.ibl_intensity = int(intensity)
        self._profiles.selected_text = Settings.CUSTOM_PROFILE_NAME
        self._apply_settings()

    def _on_sun_intensity(self, intensity):
        self.settings.sun_intensity = int(intensity)
        self._profiles.selected_text = Settings.CUSTOM_PROFILE_NAME
        self._apply_settings()

    def _on_sun_dir(self, sun_dir):
        self.settings.sun_dir = sun_dir
        self._profiles.selected_text = Settings.CUSTOM_PROFILE_NAME
        self._apply_settings()

    def _on_sun_color(self, color):
        self.settings.sun_color = color
        self._apply_settings()

    def _on_shader(self, name, index):
        self.settings.set_material(AppWindow.MATERIAL_SHADERS[index])
        self._apply_settings()

    def _on_material_prefab(self, name, index):
        self.settings.apply_material_prefab(name)
        self.settings.apply_material = True
        self._apply_settings()

    def _on_material_color(self, color):
        self.settings.material.base_color = [
            color.red,
            color.green,
            color.blue,
            color.alpha,
        ]
        self.settings.apply_material = True
        self._apply_settings()

    def _on_point_size(self, size):
        self.settings.material.point_size = int(size)
        self.settings.apply_material = True
        self._apply_settings()

    def _on_menu_open(self):
        dlg = gui.FileDialog(
            gui.FileDialog.OPEN, "Choose file to load", self.window.theme
        )
        dlg.add_filter(
            ".ply .stl .fbx .obj .off .gltf .glb",
            "Triangle mesh files (.ply, .stl, .fbx, .obj, .off, " ".gltf, .glb)",
        )
        dlg.add_filter(
            ".xyz .xyzn .xyzrgb .ply .pcd .pts",
            "Point cloud files (.xyz, .xyzn, .xyzrgb, .ply, " ".pcd, .pts)",
        )
        dlg.add_filter(".ply", "Polygon files (.ply)")
        dlg.add_filter(".stl", "Stereolithography files (.stl)")
        dlg.add_filter(".fbx", "Autodesk Filmbox files (.fbx)")
        dlg.add_filter(".obj", "Wavefront OBJ files (.obj)")
        dlg.add_filter(".off", "Object file format (.off)")
        dlg.add_filter(".gltf", "OpenGL transfer files (.gltf)")
        dlg.add_filter(".glb", "OpenGL binary transfer files (.glb)")
        dlg.add_filter(".xyz", "ASCII point cloud files (.xyz)")
        dlg.add_filter(".xyzn", "ASCII point cloud with normals (.xyzn)")
        dlg.add_filter(".xyzrgb", "ASCII point cloud files with colors (.xyzrgb)")
        dlg.add_filter(".pcd", "Point Cloud Data files (.pcd)")
        dlg.add_filter(".pts", "3D Points files (.pts)")
        dlg.add_filter("", "All files")

        # A file dialog MUST define on_cancel and on_done functions
        dlg.set_on_cancel(self._on_file_dialog_cancel)
        dlg.set_on_done(self._on_load_dialog_done)
        self.window.show_dialog(dlg)

    def _on_file_dialog_cancel(self):
        self.window.close_dialog()

    def _on_load_dialog_done(self, filename):
        self.window.close_dialog()
        self.load(filename)

    def _on_menu_export(self):
        dlg = gui.FileDialog(
            gui.FileDialog.SAVE, "Choose file to save", self.window.theme
        )
        dlg.add_filter(".png", "PNG files (.png)")
        dlg.set_on_cancel(self._on_file_dialog_cancel)
        dlg.set_on_done(self._on_export_dialog_done)
        self.window.show_dialog(dlg)

    def _on_export_dialog_done(self, filename):
        self.window.close_dialog()
        frame = self._scene.frame
        self.export_image(filename, frame.width, frame.height)

    def _on_menu_quit(self):
        gui.Application.instance.quit()

    def _on_menu_toggle_settings_panel(self):
        self._settings_panel.visible = not self._settings_panel.visible
        gui.Application.instance.menubar.set_checked(
            AppWindow.MENU_SHOW_SETTINGS, self._settings_panel.visible
        )

    def _on_menu_about(self):
        # Show a simple dialog. Although the Dialog is actually a widget, you can
        # treat it similar to a Window for layout and put all the widgets in a
        # layout which you make the only child of the Dialog.
        em = self.window.theme.font_size
        dlg = gui.Dialog("About")

        # Add the text
        dlg_layout = gui.Vert(em, gui.Margins(em, em, em, em))
        dlg_layout.add_child(gui.Label("Open3D GUI Example"))

        # Add the Ok button. We need to define a callback function to handle
        # the click.
        ok = gui.Button("OK")
        ok.set_on_clicked(self._on_about_ok)

        # We want the Ok button to be an the right side, so we need to add
        # a stretch item to the layout, otherwise the button will be the size
        # of the entire row. A stretch item takes up as much space as it can,
        # which forces the button to be its minimum size.
        h = gui.Horiz()
        h.add_stretch()
        h.add_child(ok)
        h.add_stretch()
        dlg_layout.add_child(h)

        dlg.add_child(dlg_layout)
        self.window.show_dialog(dlg)

    def _on_about_ok(self):
        self.window.close_dialog()

    def load(self, path):
        self._scene.scene.clear_geometry()

        geometry = None
        geometry_type = o3d.io.read_file_geometry_type(path)

        mesh = None
        if geometry_type & o3d.io.CONTAINS_TRIANGLES:
            mesh = o3d.io.read_triangle_mesh(path)
        if mesh is not None:
            if len(mesh.triangles) == 0:
                print("[WARNING] Contains 0 triangles, will read as point cloud")
                mesh = None
            else:
                mesh.compute_vertex_normals()
                if len(mesh.vertex_colors) == 0:
                    mesh.paint_uniform_color([1, 1, 1])
                geometry = mesh
                # Make sure the mesh has texture coordinates
                if not mesh.has_triangle_uvs():
                    uv = np.zeros((3 * len(mesh.triangles), 2), dtype=np.float64)
                    mesh.triangle_uvs = o3d.utility.Vector2dVector(uv)
        else:
            print("[Info]", path, "appears to be a point cloud")

        if geometry is None:
            cloud = None
            try:
                cloud = o3d.io.read_point_cloud(path)
            except Exception:
                pass
            if cloud is not None:
                print("[Info] Successfully read", path)
                if not cloud.has_normals():
                    cloud.estimate_normals()
                cloud.normalize_normals()
                geometry = cloud
            else:
                print("[WARNING] Failed to read points", path)

        if geometry is not None:
            try:
                self._scene.scene.add_geometry(
                    "__model__", geometry, self.settings.material
                )
                bounds = geometry.get_axis_aligned_bounding_box()
                self._scene.setup_camera(60, bounds, bounds.get_center())
            except Exception as e:
                print(e)

    def export_image(self, path, width, height):
        def on_image(image):
            img = image

            quality = 9  # png
            if path.endswith(".jpg"):
                quality = 100
            o3d.io.write_image(path, img, quality)

        self._scene.scene.scene.render_to_image(on_image)


def main():
    # We need to initalize the application, which finds the necessary shaders
    # for rendering and prepares the cross-platform window abstraction.
    gui.Application.instance.initialize()

    w = AppWindow(1024, 768)

    if len(sys.argv) > 1:
        path = sys.argv[1]
        if os.path.exists(path):
            w.load(path)
        else:
            w.window.show_message_box("Error", "Could not open file '" + path + "'")

    # Run the event loop. This will not return until the last window is closed.
    gui.Application.instance.run()


if __name__ == "__main__":
    main()
```

### cupmb/backend/__init__.py
```
#
# Copyright (c) 2021-2026 Jean-Baptiste Keck <jbkeck@hotmail.com>
# SPDX-License-Identifier: MIT
#
import os

from cupmb.enums import Backend


def find_cuda_include_dirs(logger, extra_candidates=()):
    """
    Determine the CUDA toolkit include directories (used to build kernels against the CUDA standard library).

    The CUDA root is determined from the CUPMB_CUDA_ROOT environment variable if it is set. Otherwise, the
    first existing directory among extra_candidates (backend specific), CUDA_PATH, CUDA_HOME and /usr/local/cuda
    is used. For CUDA >= 13 toolkits, the include/cccl directory is returned as well when it exists.
    """
    if "CUPMB_CUDA_ROOT" in os.environ:
        cuda_root = os.environ["CUPMB_CUDA_ROOT"].strip()
        if not os.path.isdir(cuda_root):
            msg = f"CUPMB_CUDA_ROOT has been set to '{cuda_root}' but this directory does not exist."
            logger.error(msg)
            raise RuntimeError(msg)
    else:
        candidates = tuple(extra_candidates) + (
            ("CUDA_PATH", os.environ.get("CUDA_PATH")),
            ("CUDA_HOME", os.environ.get("CUDA_HOME")),
            ("default CUDA root", "/usr/local/cuda"),
        )
        cuda_root = None
        for source, candidate in candidates:
            if not candidate:
                continue
            candidate = candidate.strip()
            if os.path.isdir(os.path.join(candidate, "include")):
                logger.debug(f"Using CUDA root '{candidate}' from {source}.")
                cuda_root = candidate
                break
            logger.debug(f"Ignoring CUDA root '{candidate}' from {source} (no include directory).")
        if cuda_root is None:
            msg = (
                "Could not determine the CUDA toolkit root directory, please set the CUPMB_CUDA_ROOT "
                "(or CUDA_PATH) environment variable to your CUDA toolkit root directory."
            )
            logger.error(msg)
            raise RuntimeError(msg)

    cuda_include_dir = os.path.join(cuda_root, "include")
    if not os.path.isdir(cuda_include_dir):
        msg = (
            f"Could not find CUDA include directory '{cuda_include_dir}', please set the CUPMB_CUDA_ROOT "
            "environment variable to your CUDA toolkit root directory."
        )
        logger.error(msg)
        raise RuntimeError(msg)

    include_dirs = [cuda_include_dir]
    cccl_include_dir = os.path.join(cuda_include_dir, "cccl")
    if os.path.isdir(cccl_include_dir):
        # CUDA >= 13 toolkits ship libcu++/CUB/Thrust headers in include/cccl
        include_dirs.append(cccl_include_dir)

    return tuple(map(os.path.realpath, include_dirs))


class CudaBackend:
    def __new__(cls, backend, initialize_context=True):
        if backend is Backend.CUPY:
            from cupmb.backend.cupy_backend import CupyBackend

            return CupyBackend
        elif backend is Backend.PYCUDA:
            from cupmb.backend.pycuda_backend import PyCudaBackend

            if initialize_context:
                import pycuda.autoinit  # noqa: F401
            return PyCudaBackend
        else:
            raise NotImplementedError(backend)

    @classmethod
    def determine_cuda_include_dirs(cls, logger):
        return find_cuda_include_dirs(logger)

    @classmethod
    def determine_cuda_include_dir(cls, logger):
        return cls.determine_cuda_include_dirs(logger)[0]
```

### cupmb/backend/cupy_backend.py
```
#
# Copyright (c) 2021-2026 Jean-Baptiste Keck <jbkeck@hotmail.com>
# SPDX-License-Identifier: MIT
#
import tempfile, threading, functools

from cupmb.backend import CudaBackend, find_cuda_include_dirs
from cupmb.utils.logger import logger

logger.info("Importing CuPy backend.")

import cupy as cp
import numpy as np

_compiler_lock = threading.Lock()


@functools.cache
def _cudart():
    # The CUDA runtime python bindings are only required for OpenGL interoperability,
    # import them lazily (cuda-bindings layout first, then legacy cuda-python layout).
    try:
        from cuda.bindings import runtime as cudart
    except ImportError:
        try:
            from cuda import cudart
        except ImportError as e:
            msg = (
                "CUDA OpenGL interoperability with the CuPy backend requires the cuda-python "
                f"package (pip install cuda-python): {e}"
            )
            logger.error(msg)
            raise ImportError(msg) from e
    return cudart


def _extract_cuda_version(v):
    return f"{v // 1000}.{(v % 1000) // 10}"


int_driver_version = cp.cuda.runtime.driverGetVersion()
int_cuda_version = cp.cuda.runtime.runtimeGetVersion()
driver_version = _extract_cuda_version(int_driver_version)
cuda_version = _extract_cuda_version(int_cuda_version)

if int_driver_version < 11000:
    msg = (
        "You are using an outdated NVIDIA driver, expected a CUDA >= 11.0 compatible driver but found version "
        f"{driver_version} ({int_driver_version}). Please upgrade your NVidia driver to the latest stable "
        "driver supported by your device at https://www.nvidia.fr/Download/index.aspx."
    )
    logger.warning(msg)

if int_cuda_version < 11000:
    msg = (
        "You are using an outdated CUDA version, expected at least CUDA 11.0 but found version "
        f"{cuda_version} ({int_cuda_version}). Please download and install lastest CUDA release from "
        "https://developer.nvidia.com/cuda-downloads and upgrade cupy."
    )
    logger.warning(msg)

msg = (
    f"Loaded cuda runtime {cuda_version} ({int_cuda_version}), "
    f"driver version is {driver_version} ({int_driver_version})."
)
logger.info(msg)


def format_cudart_err(err):
    cudart = _cudart()
    return (
        f"{cudart.cudaGetErrorName(err)[1].decode('utf-8')}({int(err)}): "
        f"{cudart.cudaGetErrorString(err)[1].decode('utf-8')}"
    )


def check_cudart_err(args):
    if isinstance(args, tuple):
        assert len(args) >= 1
        err = args[0]
        if len(args) == 1:
            ret = None
        elif len(args) == 2:
            ret = args[1]
        else:
            ret = args[1:]
    else:
        ret = None

    cudart = _cudart()
    assert isinstance(err, cudart.cudaError_t), type(err)
    if err != cudart.cudaError_t.cudaSuccess:
        raise RuntimeError(format_cudart_err(err))

    return ret


class CupyDevice:
    """
    CuPy device wrapper. Instances are cached per device id because querying device
    properties and attributes is expensive (this used to dominate host overhead).
    """

    _instances = {}
    _instances_lock = threading.Lock()

    def __new__(cls, device_id):
        device_id = int(device_id)
        with cls._instances_lock:
            instance = cls._instances.get(device_id)
            if instance is None:
                instance = super().__new__(cls)
                instance._initialize(device_id)
                cls._instances[device_id] = instance
        return instance

    def _initialize(self, device_id):
        self._cupy_device = cp.cuda.Device(device_id)
        self._device_props = cp.cuda.runtime.getDeviceProperties(device_id)
        self._device_attrs = self._cupy_device.attributes
        self._pci_bus_id = self._cupy_device.pci_bus_id
        self._tls = threading.local()

    def __enter__(self):
        # thread-safe and reentrant equivalent of cupy.cuda.Device.__enter__
        previous_device_id = cp.cuda.runtime.getDevice()
        self._tls.__dict__.setdefault("stack", []).append(previous_device_id)
        if previous_device_id != self.id:
            cp.cuda.runtime.setDevice(self.id)
        return self

    def __exit__(self, exc_type, exc_val, exc_tb):
        previous_device_id = self._tls.stack.pop()
        if previous_device_id != self.id:
            cp.cuda.runtime.setDevice(previous_device_id)

    @property
    def handle(self):
        return self._cupy_device

    @property
    def id(self):
        return self._cupy_device.id

    @property
    def max_grid_dim(self):
        return (self.max_grid_dim_x, self.max_grid_dim_y, self.max_grid_dim_z)

    @property
    def max_block_dim(self):
        return (self.max_block_dim_x, self.max_block_dim_y, self.max_block_dim_z)

    @property
    def max_threads_per_multiprocessor(self):
        return self._device_props["maxThreadsPerMultiProcessor"]

    @property
    def max_blocks_per_multiprocessor(self):
        return self._device_props["maxBlocksPerMultiProcessor"]

    @property
    def multiprocessor_count(self):
        return self._device_props["multiProcessorCount"]

    @property
    def major(self):
        return self._device_props["major"]

    @property
    def minor(self):
        return self._device_props["minor"]

    @property
    def name(self):
        return self._device_props["name"].decode("utf-8")

    @property
    def pci_bus_id(self):
        return self._pci_bus_id

    @property
    def warp_size(self):
        return self._device_attrs["WarpSize"]

    @property
    def max_threads_per_block(self):
        return self._device_attrs["MaxThreadsPerBlock"]

    @property
    def max_block_dim_x(self):
        return self._device_attrs["MaxBlockDimX"]

    @property
    def max_block_dim_y(self):
        return self._device_attrs["MaxBlockDimY"]

    @property
    def max_block_dim_z(self):
        return self._device_attrs["MaxBlockDimZ"]

    @property
    def max_grid_dim_x(self):
        return self._device_attrs["MaxGridDimX"]

    @property
    def max_grid_dim_y(self):
        return self._device_attrs["MaxGridDimY"]

    @property
    def max_grid_dim_z(self):
        return self._device_attrs["MaxGridDimZ"]

    @property
    def max_shared_memory_per_block(self):
        return self._device_attrs["MaxSharedMemoryPerBlock"]

    @property
    def max_shared_memory_per_block_optin(self):
        return self._device_attrs["MaxSharedMemoryPerBlockOptin"]

    @property
    def free_memory_bytes(self):
        return self._cupy_device.mem_info[0]

    @property
    def global_memory_bytes(self):
        return self._device_props["totalGlobalMem"]


class CupyKernel:
    def __init__(self, handle):
        assert isinstance(handle, cp.RawKernel), type(handle)
        self._cupy_kernel = handle

    @property
    def handle(self):
        return self._cupy_kernel

    @property
    def max_threads_per_block(self):
        return self._cupy_kernel.max_threads_per_block

    def __call__(self, grid, block, args, stream, shared_mem=None, time_kernel=False):
        shared_mem = shared_mem if (shared_mem is not None) else 0
        if time_kernel:
            start = stream.record()
            with stream:
                self._cupy_kernel(grid, block, args, shared_mem=shared_mem)
            end = stream.record()
            stream.synchronize()
            return 1e-3 * cp.cuda.get_elapsed_time(start, end)
        else:
            with stream:
                self._cupy_kernel(grid, block, args, shared_mem=shared_mem)


class CudaOpenGLMappedBuffer:
    def __init__(self, gl_buffer, flags=0):
        self._gl_buffer = int(gl_buffer)
        self._flags = int(flags)

        self._graphics_ressource = None
        self._cuda_buffer = None

        self.register()

    @property
    def gl_buffer(self):
        return self._gl_buffer

    @property
    def cuda_buffer(self):
        assert self.mapped
        return self._cuda_buffer

    @property
    def graphics_ressource(self):
        assert self.registered
        return self._graphics_ressource

    @property
    def registered(self):
        return self._graphics_ressource is not None

    @property
    def mapped(self):
        return self._cuda_buffer is not None

    def __enter__(self):
        return self.map()

    def __exit__(self, exc_type, exc_value, trace):
        self.unmap()
        return False

    def __del__(self):
        self.unregister()

    @staticmethod
    def _stream_ptr(stream):
        return 0 if (stream is None) else stream.ptr

    def register(self):
        if not self.registered:
            self._graphics_ressource = check_cudart_err(
                _cudart().cudaGraphicsGLRegisterBuffer(self._gl_buffer, self._flags)
            )
        return self._graphics_ressource

    def unregister(self):
        if self.registered:
            self.unmap()
            self._graphics_ressource = check_cudart_err(
                _cudart().cudaGraphicsUnregisterResource(self._graphics_ressource)
            )
        return self

    def map(self, stream=None):
        if not self.registered:
            raise RuntimeError("Cannot map an unregistered buffer.")

        if not self.mapped:
            cudart = _cudart()
            check_cudart_err(cudart.cudaGraphicsMapResources(1, self._graphics_ressource, self._stream_ptr(stream)))

            ptr, size = check_cudart_err(cudart.cudaGraphicsResourceGetMappedPointer(self._graphics_ressource))

            self._cuda_buffer = cp.cuda.MemoryPointer(cp.cuda.UnownedMemory(ptr, size, self), 0)

        return self._cuda_buffer

    def unmap(self, stream=None):
        if not self.registered:
            raise RuntimeError("Cannot unmap an unregistered buffer.")

        if self.mapped:
            self._cuda_buffer = check_cudart_err(
                _cudart().cudaGraphicsUnmapResources(1, self._graphics_ressource, self._stream_ptr(stream))
            )

        return self


class CudaOpenGLMappedArray(CudaOpenGLMappedBuffer):
    def __init__(self, dtype, shape, gl_buffer, flags=0, strides=None, order="C"):
        super().__init__(gl_buffer, flags)
        self._dtype = dtype
        self._shape = shape
        self._strides = strides
        self._order = order

    @property
    def cuda_array(self):
        assert self.mapped
        return cp.ndarray(
            shape=self._shape,
            dtype=self._dtype,
            strides=self._strides,
            order=self._order,
            memptr=self._cuda_buffer,
        )

    def map(self, *args, **kwargs):
        super().map(*args, **kwargs)
        return self.cuda_array


class CupyBackend(CudaBackend):
    ArrayBackend = cp
    Array = cp.ndarray
    Buffer = cp.cuda.memory.MemoryPointer
    Stream = cp.cuda.Stream
    Device = CupyDevice
    Kernel = CupyKernel
    AllocationError = cp.cuda.memory.OutOfMemoryError

    CudaOpenGLMappedArray = CudaOpenGLMappedArray
    CudaOpenGLMappedBuffer = CudaOpenGLMappedBuffer

    NullMem = cp.cuda.UnownedMemory(ptr=0x0, size=0, owner=None, device_id=0)
    NullPtr = cp.cuda.memory.MemoryPointer(NullMem, 0)

    @classmethod
    def default_stream(cls):
        # honor 'with stream:' blocks of the caller
        return cp.cuda.get_current_stream()

    @classmethod
    def asnumpy(cls, a, stream=None):
        # copy is performed on (and synchronized with) the given stream
        return cp.asnumpy(a, stream=stream, blocking=True)

    @classmethod
    def asarray(cls, a):
        return cp.asarray(a)

    @classmethod
    def get_nbytes(cls, a):
        if a is None:
            return 0
        elif isinstance(a, cls.Array):
            return a.nbytes
        elif isinstance(a, cls.Buffer):
            return a.mem.size
        else:
            raise NotImplementedError(type(a))

    @classmethod
    def synchronize_stream(cls, stream):
        stream.synchronize()

    @classmethod
    def get_data_pointers(cls, *args):
        if len(args) == 1 and isinstance(args[0], tuple):
            args = args[0]
        return tuple(cls.NullPtr if (a is None) else a for a in args)

    @classmethod
    def array_from_pointer(cls, ptr, shape, dtype):
        assert isinstance(ptr, cls.Buffer)
        req_bytes = int(np.prod(shape, dtype=np.int64)) * np.dtype(dtype).itemsize
        ptr_bytes = cls.get_nbytes(ptr)
        if req_bytes > ptr_bytes:
            msg = (
                f"Buffer of {ptr_bytes} bytes is too small to hold an array of shape {shape} "
                f"and dtype {np.dtype(dtype)}."
            )
            raise ValueError(msg)
        return cls.Array(shape=shape, dtype=dtype, memptr=ptr)

    @classmethod
    def fill_zeros(cls, a, stream):
        assert isinstance(a, cls.Array), type(a)
        if a.nbytes == 0:
            return
        cp.cuda.runtime.memsetAsync(
            a.data.ptr,
            0,
            a.nbytes,
            stream.ptr,
        )

    @classmethod
    def determine_cuda_include_dirs(cls, logger):
        # cupy.cuda.get_cuda_path() may return None when no CUDA toolkit could be found
        return find_cuda_include_dirs(logger, extra_candidates=(("CuPy", cp.cuda.get_cuda_path()),))

    @classmethod
    @functools.cache
    def compiler_version(cls):
        major, minor = cp.cuda.nvrtc.getVersion()
        return f"cupy-{cp.__version__}-nvrtc-{major}.{minor}"

    @classmethod
    def build_module_and_extract_kernels_from_source(
        cls,
        logger,
        device_major,
        device_minor,
        source_code,
        kernel_names,
        build_options,
    ):
        kernels, _, _ = cls.build_kernels(logger, device_major, device_minor, source_code, kernel_names, build_options)
        return kernels

    @classmethod
    def build_kernels(
        cls,
        logger,
        device_major,
        device_minor,
        source_code,
        kernel_names,
        build_options,
    ):
        """
        Build the kernels, return the kernels, the compiled module (cubin or ptx) and the mangled kernel names.
        The compiled module is None when it could not be retrieved from CuPy (it is then not cached).
        """

        def debug_dump_kernel():
            with tempfile.NamedTemporaryFile(mode="w", prefix="cupmb_kernel_", suffix=".cu", delete=False) as f:
                f.write(source_code)
                debug_dump_file = f.name
            debug_msg = f"Build failure, kernel source code has been dumped to file://{debug_dump_file}."
            logger.error(debug_msg)

        # CuPy does not use its on disk cache when name expressions are used, and it does not expose the
        # compiled module: intercept the NVRTC compilation to retrieve it while keeping the exact same build
        # options (CuPy adds its own include directories and -ftz=true). CuPy >= 14 calls the private
        # _compile_using_nvrtc_no_warning directly, older versions call compile_using_nvrtc.
        compiler = cp.cuda.compiler
        compile_using_nvrtc_name = next(
            (
                name
                for name in ("_compile_using_nvrtc_no_warning", "compile_using_nvrtc")
                if callable(getattr(compiler, name, None))
            ),
            None,
        )
        compile_using_nvrtc = getattr(compiler, compile_using_nvrtc_name) if compile_using_nvrtc_name else None
        compiled = []

        def capture_compile_using_nvrtc(*args, **kwds):
            result = compile_using_nvrtc(*args, **kwds)
            compiled.append(result)
            return result

        with _compiler_lock:
            if compile_using_nvrtc is not None:
                setattr(compiler, compile_using_nvrtc_name, capture_compile_using_nvrtc)
            try:
                try:
                    module = cp.RawModule(
                        code=source_code,
                        name_expressions=kernel_names,
                        backend="nvrtc",
                        options=build_options,
                    )
                except Exception as e:
                    debug_dump_kernel()
                    logger.error(f"Failed to build module, build options were {build_options}.")
                    logger.error(e)
                    raise e

                try:
                    kernels = tuple(map(cls.Kernel, map(module.get_function, kernel_names)))
                except Exception as e:
                    debug_dump_kernel()
                    logger.error(f"Failed to extract kernels, build options were {build_options}.")
                    logger.error(e)
                    raise e
            finally:
                if compile_using_nvrtc is not None:
                    setattr(compiler, compile_using_nvrtc_name, compile_using_nvrtc)

        binary, mangled_kernel_names = None, None
        if len(compiled) == 1:
            try:
                binary, mapping = compiled[0]
                mangled_kernel_names = tuple(mapping[kname] for kname in kernel_names)
                if isinstance(binary, str):
                    binary = binary.encode()
                if not isinstance(binary, bytes):
                    binary = None
            except Exception:
                binary = None
        if binary is None:
            logger.debug("Could not retrieve the compiled module from CuPy, it will not be cached.")

        return kernels, binary, mangled_kernel_names

    @classmethod
    def load_kernels(cls, logger, binary_path, mangled_kernel_names):
        """Load kernels from a compiled module file (cubin or ptx) and the mangled kernel names."""
        module = cp.RawModule(path=binary_path)
        return tuple(map(cls.Kernel, map(module.get_function, mangled_kernel_names)))
```

```
import os, sys, tempfile, subprocess, threading, functools

from cupmb.backend import CudaBackend
from cupmb.utils.logger import logger
from cupmb.utils.IO import utils_dir

logger.info("Importing PyCuda backend.")

import pycuda
import pycuda.driver
import pycuda.gpuarray

import numpy as np


def _extract_cuda_version(v):
    return f"{v // 1000}.{(v % 1000) // 10}.{v%10}"


def _extract_int_version(v):
    return 1000 * v[0] + 10 * v[1] + v[2]


# Check PyCuda driver and cuda version
int_driver_version = pycuda.driver.get_driver_version()
driver_version = _extract_cuda_version(int_driver_version)

cuda_version = pycuda.driver.get_version()
int_cuda_version = _extract_int_version(cuda_version)
cuda_version = ".".join(map(str, cuda_version))

if int_driver_version < 11000:
    msg = (
        "You are using an outdated NVIDIA driver, expected a CUDA >= 11.0 compatible driver but found version "
        f"{driver_version} ({int_driver_version}). Please upgrade your NVidia driver to the latest stable "
        "driver supported by your device at https://www.nvidia.fr/Download/index.aspx."
    )
    logger.warning(msg)

if int_cuda_version < 11000:
    msg = (
        "You are using an outdated CUDA version, expected at least CUDA 11.0 but found version "
        f"{cuda_version} ({int_cuda_version}). Please download and install lastest CUDA release from "
        "https://developer.nvidia.com/cuda-downloads and rebuild PyCuda."
    )
    logger.warning(msg)

msg = (
    f"PyCuda loaded cuda runtime {cuda_version} ({int_cuda_version}), "
    f"driver version is {driver_version} ({int_driver_version})."
)
logger.info(msg)


class PyCudaDevice:
    """
    PyCuda device wrapper. Instances are cached per device id because querying device
    attributes is expensive. Entering the device makes sure that the current CUDA context
    is bound to this device (the device primary context is pushed if required).
    """

    da = pycuda.driver.device_attribute

    _instances = {}
    _instances_lock = threading.Lock()

    def __new__(cls, device_id):
        device_id = int(device_id)
        with cls._instances_lock:
            instance = cls._instances.get(device_id)
            if instance is None:
                instance = super().__new__(cls)
                instance._initialize(device_id)
                cls._instances[device_id] = instance
        return instance

    def _initialize(self, device_id):
        self._pycuda_device = pycuda.driver.Device(device_id)
        self._device_attrs = self._pycuda_device.get_attributes()
        self._device_id = device_id
        self._pci_bus_id = self._pycuda_device.pci_bus_id()
        self._compute_capability = self._pycuda_device.compute_capability()
        self._primary_context = None
        self._tls = threading.local()

    def _current_context_is_on_device(self):
        try:
            current_device = pycuda.driver.Context.get_device()
        except (pycuda.driver.LogicError, pycuda.driver.Error):
            return False
        return current_device.pci_bus_id() == self._pci_bus_id

    def __enter__(self):
        stack = self._tls.__dict__.setdefault("pushed", [])
        if self._current_context_is_on_device():
            stack.append(False)
        else:
            if self._primary_context is None:
                self._primary_context = self._pycuda_device.retain_primary_context()
            self._primary_context.push()
            stack.append(True)
        return self

    def __exit__(self, exc_type, exc_val, exc_tb):
        if self._tls.pushed.pop():
            pycuda.driver.Context.pop()

    @property
    def handle(self):
        return self._pycuda_device

    @property
    def id(self):
        return self._device_id

    @property
    def max_grid_dim(self):
        return (self.max_grid_dim_x, self.max_grid_dim_y, self.max_grid_dim_z)

    @property
    def max_block_dim(self):
        return (self.max_block_dim_x, self.max_block_dim_y, self.max_block_dim_z)

    @property
    def major(self):
        return self._compute_capability[0]

    @property
    def minor(self):
        return self._compute_capability[1]

    @property
    def name(self):
        return self._pycuda_device.name()

    @property
    def pci_bus_id(self):
        return self._pci_bus_id

    @property
    def warp_size(self):
        return self._device_attrs[self.da.WARP_SIZE]

    @property
    def max_threads_per_multiprocessor(self):
        return self._device_attrs[self.da.MAX_THREADS_PER_MULTIPROCESSOR]

    @property
    def max_blocks_per_multiprocessor(self):
        return self._device_attrs[self.da.MAX_BLOCKS_PER_MULTIPROCESSOR]

    @property
    def multiprocessor_count(self):
        return self._device_attrs[self.da.MULTIPROCESSOR_COUNT]

    @property
    def max_threads_per_block(self):
        return self._device_attrs[self.da.MAX_THREADS_PER_BLOCK]

    @property
    def max_block_dim_x(self):
        return self._device_attrs[self.da.MAX_BLOCK_DIM_X]

    @property
    def max_block_dim_y(self):
        return self._device_attrs[self.da.MAX_BLOCK_DIM_Y]

    @property
    def max_block_dim_z(self):
        return self._device_attrs[self.da.MAX_BLOCK_DIM_Z]

    @property
    def max_grid_dim_x(self):
        return self._device_attrs[self.da.MAX_GRID_DIM_X]

    @property
    def max_grid_dim_y(self):
        return self._device_attrs[self.da.MAX_GRID_DIM_Y]

    @property
    def max_grid_dim_z(self):
        return self._device_attrs[self.da.MAX_GRID_DIM_Z]

    @property
    def max_shared_memory_per_block(self):
        return self._device_attrs[self.da.MAX_SHARED_MEMORY_PER_BLOCK]

    @property
    def max_shared_memory_per_block_optin(self):
        try:
            return self._device_attrs[self.da.MAX_SHARED_MEMORY_PER_BLOCK]
        except AttributeError:
            return self.max_shared_memory_per_block

    @property
    def free_memory_bytes(self):
        with self:
            return pycuda.driver.mem_get_info()[0]

    @property
    def global_memory_bytes(self):
        return self._pycuda_device.total_memory()


class PyCudaKernel:
    def __init__(self, handle):
        assert isinstance(handle, pycuda.driver.Function), type(handle)
        self._pycuda_kernel = handle

    @property
    def handle(self):
        return self._pycuda_kernel

    @property
    def max_threads_per_block(self):
        return self._pycuda_kernel.max_threads_per_block

    def __call__(self, grid, block, args, stream=None, shared_mem=None, time_kernel=False):
        assert isinstance(grid, tuple)
        assert isinstance(block, tuple)
        assert len(grid) <= 3, len(grid)
        assert len(block) <= 3, len(block)
        grid = tuple(map(int, grid)) + (1,) * (3 - len(grid))
        block = tuple(map(int, block)) + (1,) * (3 - len(block))
        shared_mem = int(shared_mem) if (shared_mem is not None) else 0
        if time_kernel:
            # pycuda's own time_kernel records events on the null stream, record them on our stream instead
            start, end = pycuda.driver.Event(), pycuda.driver.Event()
            start.record(stream)
        self._pycuda_kernel(
            *args,
            grid=grid,
            block=block,
            shared=shared_mem,
            stream=stream,
        )
        if time_kernel:
            end.record(stream)
            end.synchronize()
            return 1e-3 * start.time_till(end)


class PointerHolderBase(pycuda.driver.PointerHolderBase):
    def __init__(self, int_ptr, size):
        super().__init__()
        self._int_ptr = int_ptr
        self._size = size

    @property
    def int_ptr(self):
        return self._int_ptr

    @property
    def size(self):
        return self._size

    def get_pointer(self):
        return self._int_ptr


@functools.cache
def _opengl_interop_classes():
    # pycuda.gl is only available when PyCuda has been built with OpenGL support (CUDA_ENABLE_GL),
    # it is thus imported lazily, only when OpenGL interoperability is actually requested.
    try:
        import pycuda.gl
    except ImportError as e:
        msg = (
            "PyCuda OpenGL interoperability is not available, please rebuild PyCuda with "
            f"OpenGL support (CUDA_ENABLE_GL=True) to use this feature ({e})."
        )
        logger.error(msg)
        raise ImportError(msg) from e

    class CudaOpenGLMappedBuffer(pycuda.gl.RegisteredBuffer):
        def __init__(self, gl_buffer, flags=0):
            super().__init__(int(gl_buffer), flags)
            self._registered_mapping = None
            self._registered = True

        @property
        def gl_buffer(self):
            return self.gl_handle()

        @property
        def cuda_buffer(self):
            assert self.mapped
            ptr, size = self._registered_mapping.device_ptr_and_size()
            return PointerHolderBase(ptr, size)

        @property
        def registered(self):
            return self._registered

        @property
        def mapped(self):
            return self._registered_mapping is not None

        def __enter__(self):
            return self.map()

        def __exit__(self, exc_type, exc_value, trace):
            self.unmap()
            return False

        def __del__(self):
            self.unregister()

        def register(self):
            assert self._registered
            return self

        def unregister(self):
            if self._registered:
                super().unregister()
                self._registered = False
            return self

        def map(self, stream=None):
            if not self.registered:
                raise RuntimeError("Cannot map an unregistered buffer.")

            if not self.mapped:
                self._registered_mapping = super().map(stream)

            return self.cuda_buffer

        def unmap(self, stream=None):
            if not self.registered:
                raise RuntimeError("Cannot unmap an unregistered buffer.")

            if self.mapped:
                self._registered_mapping.unmap(stream)
                self._registered_mapping = None

            return self

    class CudaOpenGLMappedArray(CudaOpenGLMappedBuffer):
        def __init__(self, dtype, shape, gl_buffer, flags=0, strides=None, order="C"):
            super().__init__(gl_buffer, flags)
            self._dtype = dtype
            self._shape = shape
            self._strides = strides
            self._order = order

        @property
        def cuda_array(self):
            assert self.mapped

            def allocator(nbytes, buf=self.cuda_buffer):
                assert nbytes <= buf.size, (nbytes, buf.size)
                return buf

            return pycuda.gpuarray.GPUArray(
                shape=self._shape,
                dtype=self._dtype,
                strides=self._strides,
                order=self._order,
                allocator=allocator,
            )

        def map(self, *args, **kwargs):
            super().map(*args, **kwargs)
            return self.cuda_array

    return CudaOpenGLMappedBuffer, CudaOpenGLMappedArray


class _LazyOpenGLInteropClass:
    def __init__(self, index):
        self._index = index

    def __get__(self, instance, owner):
        return _opengl_interop_classes()[self._index]


class PyCudaBackend(CudaBackend):
    ArrayBackend = pycuda.gpuarray
    Array = pycuda.gpuarray.GPUArray
    Buffer = PointerHolderBase
    Stream = pycuda.driver.Stream
    Device = PyCudaDevice
    Kernel = PyCudaKernel
    AllocationError = pycuda.driver.MemoryError

    CudaOpenGLMappedBuffer = _LazyOpenGLInteropClass(0)
    CudaOpenGLMappedArray = _LazyOpenGLInteropClass(1)

    class NullPtr:
        gpudata = 0x0

    @classmethod
    def default_stream(cls):
        # None means the (legacy) default stream for all pycuda launches and copies
        return None

    @classmethod
    def asnumpy(cls, a, stream=None):
        if stream is None:
            return a.get()
        h = a.get_async(stream=stream)
        stream.synchronize()
        return h

    @classmethod
    def asarray(cls, a):
        return pycuda.gpuarray.to_gpu(a)

    @classmethod
    def get_nbytes(cls, a):
        if a is None:
            return 0
        elif isinstance(a, cls.Array):
            return a.nbytes
        elif isinstance(a, cls.Buffer):
            return a.size
        else:
            raise NotImplementedError(type(a))

    @classmethod
    def synchronize_stream(cls, stream):
        if stream is None:
            pycuda.driver.Context.synchronize()
        else:
            stream.synchronize()

    @classmethod
    def get_data_pointers(cls, *args):
        if len(args) == 1 and isinstance(args[0], tuple):
            args = args[0]
        return tuple(cls.NullPtr if (a is None) else a for a in args)

    @classmethod
    def array_from_pointer(cls, ptr, shape, dtype):
        assert isinstance(ptr, cls.Buffer)
        req_bytes = int(np.prod(shape, dtype=np.int64)) * np.dtype(dtype).itemsize
        ptr_bytes = cls.get_nbytes(ptr)
        if req_bytes > ptr_bytes:
            msg = (
                f"Buffer of {ptr_bytes} bytes is too small to hold an array of shape {shape} "
                f"and dtype {np.dtype(dtype)}."
            )
            raise ValueError(msg)
        return cls.Array(shape=shape, dtype=dtype, gpudata=ptr)

    @classmethod
    def fill_zeros(cls, a, stream):
        assert isinstance(a, cls.Array), type(a)
        if a.nbytes > 0:
            pycuda.driver.memset_d8_async(int(a.gpudata), 0, a.nbytes, stream)

    @classmethod
    @functools.cache
    def compiler_version(cls):
        nvrtc_build_program = os.path.join(utils_dir, "nvrtc_build.py")
        result = subprocess.run(
            [sys.executable, nvrtc_build_program, "--nvrtc-version"], check=True, capture_output=True, text=True
        )
        return f"pycuda-{pycuda.VERSION_TEXT}-nvrtc-{result.stdout.strip()}"

    @classmethod
    def build_module_and_extract_kernels_from_source(
        cls,
        logger,
        device_major,
        device_minor,
        source_code,
        kernel_names,
        build_options,
    ):
        kernels, _, _ = cls.build_kernels(logger, device_major, device_minor, source_code, kernel_names, build_options)
        return kernels

    @classmethod
    def build_kernels(
        cls,
        logger,
        device_major,
        device_minor,
        source_code,
        kernel_names,
        build_options,
    ):
        """
        Build the kernels, return the kernels, the compiled module (cubin or ptx) and the mangled kernel names.
        """
        # Here we use the nvrtc module because pycuda cannot handle templated __global__ functions
        # due to name mangling (see https://wiki.tiker.net/PyCuda/Examples/C++FunctionTemplates).
        # We use a subprocess to generate a ptx or cubin file that we then load back with pycuda
        # to avoid any eventual cuda context clash due to the use of official nvidia cuda python
        # modules. Please note that the subprocess also generates the list of mangled kernel names.
        # We use temporary files to communicate build options and kernel names to the subprocess,
        # because it is easier than via command line arguments. The same technique is used to get
        # back kernel ptx/cubin binary data and mangled kernel names back from the subprocess.

        nvrtc_build_program = os.path.join(utils_dir, "nvrtc_build.py")
        if not os.path.isfile(nvrtc_build_program):
            msg = f"nvrtc build program file:///{nvrtc_build_program} is missing."
            logger.error(msg)
            raise RuntimeError(msg)

        with tempfile.TemporaryDirectory() as tmpdir:
            input_knames = os.path.join(tmpdir, "input_knames")
            input_options = os.path.join(tmpdir, "input_options")
            output_knames = os.path.join(tmpdir, "output_knames")
            output_binary = os.path.join(tmpdir, "output_binary")
            source_file = os.path.join(tmpdir, "source.cu")

            with open(input_options, "w") as f:
                for opt in build_options:
                    f.write(opt)
                    f.write(os.linesep)

            with open(input_knames, "w") as f:
                for kname in kernel_names:
                    f.write(kname)
                    f.write(os.linesep)

            with open(source_file, "w") as f:
                f.write(source_code)

            args = [
                sys.executable,
                nvrtc_build_program,
                "--major",
                str(device_major),
                "--minor",
                str(device_minor),
                "--source-file",
                source_file,
                "--input-knames",
                input_knames,
                "--input-options",
                input_options,
                "--output-knames",
                output_knames,
                "--output-binary",
                output_binary,
            ]

            try:
                subprocess.run(args, check=True)
            except Exception:
                cmd = " ".join(args)
                logger.error(cmd)
                logger.error("Failed to build kernels by using nvrtc_build.py")
                raise

            if not os.path.isfile(output_knames):
                msg = f"Source file:///{output_knames} has not been generated."
                logger.error(msg)
                raise RuntimeError(msg)

            with open(output_knames) as f:
                mangled_kernel_names = tuple(filter(len, f.read().split(os.linesep)))

            if not os.path.isfile(output_binary):
                msg = f"Source file:///{output_binary} has not been generated."
                logger.error(msg)
                raise RuntimeError(msg)

            with open(output_binary, "rb") as f:
                binary = f.read()

        module = pycuda.driver.module_from_buffer(binary)
        kernels = tuple(map(cls.Kernel, map(module.get_function, mangled_kernel_names)))
        return kernels, binary, mangled_kernel_names

    @classmethod
    def load_kernels(cls, logger, binary_path, mangled_kernel_names):
        """Load kernels from a compiled module file (cubin or ptx) and the mangled kernel names."""
        with open(binary_path, "rb") as f:
            module = pycuda.driver.module_from_buffer(f.read())
        return tuple(map(cls.Kernel, map(module.get_function, mangled_kernel_names)))
```

### cupmb/kernels/pmb.cuh
```
/*
 * Copyright (c) 2021-2026 Jean-Baptiste Keck <jbkeck@hotmail.com>
 * SPDX-License-Identifier: MIT
```
